THEO. If he could live without eating, of course it would. But as things stand, he must eat to live, and so we advise really earnest students to eat food that will least clog and weigh down their brains and bodies, and will have the smallest effect in hampering and delaying the development of their intuition, their inner faculties, and powers.

ENQ. Then you do not adopt all the arguments that vegetarians in general are in the habit of using?

THEO. Certainly not. Some of their arguments are very weak and often based on assumptions that are quite false. But on the other hand, many of the things they say are quite true. For instance, we believe that much disease—and especially the great predisposition to disease that is becoming such a marked feature of our time—is very largely due to eating meat, and especially canned meats. But it would take too long to go thoroughly into this question of vegetarianism on its own merits; so please pass on to something else.

ENQ. One more question. What are the members of your Inner Section to do regarding their food when they are ill?

THEO. Follow the best practical advice they can get, of course. Don’t you grasp yet that we never impose any hard-and-fast obligations in this respect? Remember once and for all that in all such questions we take a rational, and never a fanatical, view of things. If due to illness or long habit a person cannot go without meat, why, by all means let them eat it. It is no crime; it will only slow their progress a little. After all is said and done, purely bodily actions and functions are of far less importance than what a person thinks and *feels*, what

ENQ. Then with regard to the use of wine and spirits, I suppose you do not advise people to drink them?

THEO. They are worse for his moral and spiritual growth than meat, for alcohol in all its forms has a direct, marked, and very damaging influence on man's psychic condition. Wine and spirit drinking is only slightly less destructive to the development of the inner powers than the habitual use of hashish, opium, and similar drugs.

### *THEOSOPHY AND MARRIAGE.*

ENQ. Now to another question; must a man marry or remain a celibate?

THEO. It depends on the kind of man you mean. If you refer to one who intends to live in the world—one who, even though a good, earnest Theosophist and an ardent worker for our cause, still has ties and wishes which bind him to the world; who, in short, does not feel that he is finished forever with what men call life, and that he desires one thing and one thing only—to know the truth and to be able to help others—then for such a person I say there is no reason why he should not marry, if he likes to take the risks of that lottery where there are so many more blanks than prizes. Surely you cannot believe us so absurd and fanatical as to preach against marriage altogether? On the contrary, except in a few exceptional cases of practical <term>Occultism</term>, marriage is the only remedy against immorality.

ENQ. But why can one not acquire this knowledge and power while living a married life?

THEO. My dear sir, I cannot discuss physiological questions with you; but I can give you an obvious and, I think, a sufficient answer that will explain the moral reasons we provide for it. Can a man serve two masters? No! Then it is equally impossible for him to divide his attention between the pursuit of <term>Occultism</term> and a wife. If he tries to do so, he will certainly fail to do either properly. Let me remind you, practical <term>Occultism</term> is far too serious and dangerous a study for a man to undertake unless he is in the most deadly earnest and ready to sacrifice *everything*—himself *first of all*—to achieve his goal. But this does not apply to the members of our Inner Section. I am only referring to those who are determined to walk the path of discipleship that leads to the highest goal. Most, if not all, of those who join our Inner Section are only beginners, preparing themselves in this life to truly enter that path in future lives.

---

## THEOSOPHY AND EDUCATION.

ENQ. One of your strongest arguments for the inadequacy of the existing forms of religion in the West—as well as the materialistic philosophy that is now so popular, but which you seem to consider an "abomination of desolation"—is the large amount of misery and wretchedness that undeniably exists, especially in our great cities. But surely you must recognize how much has been and is being done to remedy this state of affairs through the spread of education and the diffusion of intelligence.

THEO. Future generations will hardly thank you for such a "diffusion of intelligence," nor will your present education do much good for the poor starving masses.

ENQ. Ah! But you must give us time. It is only a few years since we began to educate the people.

THEO. ENQ. Well, you may be right; but now—

THEO. Just let us consider this question of education from a broad standpoint, and I will prove to you that you are doing harm, not good, with many of your boasted improvements. The schools for the poorer children, though far less useful than they ought to be, are good in contrast with the vile surroundings to which they are doomed by your modern Society. The *infusion of a little practical Theosophy* would help the poor suffering masses a hundred times more in life than all this infusion of (useless) intelligence.

Very little experience with examinations is enough to show that the education they produce is simply a training of the physical memory, and, sooner or later, all your schools will sink to this level. As for any real, sound cultivation of the thinking and reasoning power, it is simply impossible while everything has to be judged by the results as tested by competitive examinations. Again, school training is of the greatest importance in forming character, especially in its moral bearing. Now, from first to last, your modern system is based on the so-called scientific revelations: "The struggle for existence" and the "survival of the fittest." All through his early life, every man has these driven into him by practical example and experience, as well as by direct teaching, until it is impossible to eradicate from his mind the idea that "self"—the lower, personal, animal self—is the be-all and end-all of life. **THEO.** Added to this is a smattering of selected, so-called Biblical facts, from the study of which all intellect is removed. It is simply a memory lesson; the "Why" of the teacher is a "Why" of circumstances and not of reason.

### *WHY, THEN, IS THERE SO MUCH PREJUDICE AGAINST THE T.S.?*

**ENQ.** If Theosophy is even half of what you say, why should there exist such a terrible ill-feeling against it? This is even more of a problem than anything else.

**THEO.** It is; but you must bear in mind how many powerful adversaries we have aroused ever since the formation of our Society. As I just said, if the Theosophical movement were one of those numerous modern crazes—as harmless in the end as they are short-lived—it would be simply laughed at (as it is now by those who still do not understand its real purpose) and left severely alone. But it is nothing of the kind. Intrinsically, Theosophy is the most serious movement of this age; and one, moreover, which threatens the very life of most of the time-honored shams, prejudices, and social evils of the day—those evils which fatten and make happy the upper

THEO. Of course I can. Lastly, we must count the defection of various prominent (?) members, for reasons I have already explained, all of whom have contributed their utmost to increase the prejudice against us.

ENQ. Can you not give me more details about these, so that I may know what to answer when asked—a brief history of the Society, in short; and why the world believes all this?
THEO. The reason is simple. Most outsiders knew absolutely nothing of the Society itself, its motives, objectives, or beliefs. From its very beginning, the world has seen in Theosophy nothing but certain marvelous phenomena, in which two-thirds of the non-spiritualists do not believe. Very soon the Society came to be regarded as a body claiming to possess "miraculous" powers. The world never realized

that the Society taught absolute disbelief in *miracles* or even the possibility of such things; that in the Society there were only a few people who possessed such psychic powers and only a few who cared for them. Nor did the world understand that the phenomena were never produced publicly, but only privately for friends, and were merely given as an accessory to prove by direct demonstration that such things could be produced without dark rooms, spirits, mediums, or any of the usual paraphernalia. Unfortunately, this misconception was greatly strengthened and exaggerated by the first book on the subject which excited much attention in Europe—Mr. Sinnett’s “*Occult World*.” If this work did much to bring the Society into prominence, it attracted still more public condemnation, derision, and misrepresentation upon the unfortunate heroes and heroine of the book. Of this, the author was more than warned in the *Occult World*, but he did not pay attention to the *prophecy*—for such it was, though half-veiled.

**ENQ.** For what reason, and since when, have the Spiritualists hated you?

**THEO.** From the first day of the Society’s existence. As soon as the fact became known that, as a body, the T.S. did not believe in communications with the spirits of the dead, but regarded the so-called “spirits” as, for the most part, astral reflections of disembodied personalities, <term>shells</term><note>In Theosophical terms, "shells" refers to the astral remains of a deceased person that lack the higher spiritual soul.</note>, etc., the Spiritualists conceived a violent hatred for us and especially for the Founders. This hatred found expression in every kind of slander, uncharitable personal remarks, and absurd misrepresentations of the Theosophical teachings in all the American Spiritualist publications. For years we were persecuted, denounced, and

abused. This began in 1875 and continues to the present day. In 1879, the headquarters of the T.S. were transferred from New York to Bombay, India, and then permanently to Madras. When the first branch of our Society, the British T.S., was founded in London, the English Spiritualists took up arms against us, just as the Americans had done; and the French Spiritists followed suit.

**ENQ.** But why should the clergy be hostile to you when, after all, the main tendency of the Theosophical doctrines is opposed to Materialism—the great enemy of all forms of religion in our day?

**THEO.** The clergy opposed us on the general principle that “He who is not with me is against me.” Since Theosophy does not agree with any one sect or creed, it is considered the enemy of all alike because it teaches that they are all, more or less, mistaken. The missionaries in India hated and tried to crush us because they saw the finest of the educated Indian youth and the <term>Brahmins</term>, who are almost inaccessible to them, joining the Society in large numbers. And yet, apart from this general class hatred, the T.S. counts in its ranks many clergymen and even one or two bishops.

**ENQ.** And what led the S.P.R. to take the field against you? You were both pursuing the same line of study in some respects, and several of the Psychic Researchers belonged to your society.

**THEO.** First of all, we were very good friends with the leaders of the S.P.R.; but when the attack on the phenomena appeared in the *Christian College Magazine*, supported by the pretended revelations of a servant, the S.P.R. found that they had compromised themselves by publishing in their “Proceedings” too

many of the phenomena that had occurred in connection with the T.S. Their ambition is to pose as an *authoritative* and strictly scientific body; so they had to choose between retaining that position by throwing the T.S. overboard and even trying to destroy it, or seeing themselves merged, in the opinion of the Sadducees <note>A reference to skeptics or materialists who deny the existence of the soul or afterlife</note> of the *grand monde* <note>high society</note>, with the “credulous” Theosophists and Spiritualists. There was no way out for them, no two choices, and they chose to throw us overboard. It was a matter of dire necessity for them. But they were so hard-pressed to find any apparently reasonable motive for the life of devotion and ceaseless labor led by the two Founders, and for the complete absence of any financial profit or other advantage to them, that our enemies were obliged to resort to the thrice-absurd, eminently ridiculous, and now famous “Russian spy theory,” to explain this devotion. But the old saying, “The blood of the martyrs is the seed of the Church,” proved once more correct. After the first shock of this attack, the T.S. doubled and tripled its numbers, but the bad impression produced still remains. A French author was right in saying, “*Calomniez, calomniez toujours et encore, il en restera toujours quelque chose.*” <note>“Slander, slander always and again; something of it will always remain.”</note> Therefore, unjust prejudices are current, and everything connected with the T.S., and especially with its Founders, is so falsely distorted because it is based on malicious hearsay alone.

**ENQ.** Yet in the 14 years during which the Society has existed, you must have had ample time and opportunity to show yourselves and your work in their true light?

**THEO.** How, or when, have we been given such an opportunity?

--- Page 293 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how the Theosophical Society was misrepresented and the difficulty of clearing its reputation after attacks from groups like the S.P.R.</meta>

<page-num>277</page-num>
->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

Our most prominent members had a strong dislike for anything that looked like publicly defending themselves. Their policy has always been: “We must live it down;” and “What does it matter what the newspapers say, or what people think?” The Society was too poor to send out public lecturers; therefore, the explanations of our views and doctrines were limited to a few Theosophical works that were successful, but which people often misunderstood or only knew about through hearsay. Our journals were, and still are, boycotted; our literary works are ignored; and to this day, no one even seems to feel quite certain whether the <term>Theosophists</term> are a kind of serpent-and-devil worshippers, or simply “<term>Esoteric Buddhists</term>”—whatever that may mean. It was useless for us to go on denying, day after day and year after year, every kind of unbelievable and nonsensical story about us; for, no sooner was one dismissed than another, even more absurd and malicious, was born from the ashes of the first. Unfortunately, human nature is so built that any good said of a person is immediately forgotten and never repeated. But one only has to utter a slander, or to start a story—no matter how absurd, false, or incredible it may be, as long as it is connected with some unpopular person—for it to be successful and immediately accepted as a historical fact. Like *Don Basilio’s* <note>A character in the play and opera "The Barber of Seville" who describes how a small spark of slander can grow into a roar.</note> “CALUMNY,” the rumor springs up, at first, as a soft gentle breeze hardly stirring the grass under your feet, and arising from an unknown source; then, in the shortest amount of time, it is transformed into a strong wind, begins to blow a gale, and immediately becomes a roaring storm!

<meta>This page continues the dialogue regarding the nature of rumors and slander and how they have affected the reputation of the Theosophical Society.</meta>

<page-num>278</page-num>
->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

Slander among news is what an octopus is among fish; it sucks into one’s mind and fastens upon our memory, which feeds upon it, leaving permanent marks even after the slander itself has been physically destroyed. A slanderous lie is the only master key that will open any and every brain. It is sure to be welcomed and hosted in every human mind, the highest as well as the lowest, if it is even slightly prejudiced, no matter how low the source or motive from which it began.

<term>ENQ.</term> Don’t you think your assertion is altogether too sweeping? The Englishman has never been too quick to believe everything said, and our nation is proverbially known for its love of fair play. A lie has no legs to stand on for long, and—

<term>THEO.</term> The Englishman is as ready to believe evil as a person of any other nation; for it is human nature, and not a national characteristic. As for lies, if they have no legs to stand on, according to the proverb, they have incredibly fast wings; and they can and do fly farther and wider than any other kind of news, in England as elsewhere. Remember that lies and slander are the only kind of literature we can always get for free, and without paying any subscription. We can make the experiment if you like. Will you, who are so interested in <term>Theosophical</term> matters and have heard so much about us, ask me questions about as many of these rumors and "hearsays" as you can think of? I will answer you with the truth, and nothing but the truth, subject to the strictest verification.

<term>ENQ.</term> Before we change the subject, let us have the whole truth on this one. Now, some writers have called your teachings "immoral and harmful"; others, on the grounds that many so-called "authorities"

They say that since modern Theosophy is so closely allied with Eastern, and particularly Indian, thought, it cannot be free from this taint. <term>THEO.</term> I have heard and read about this before; and I answer that no more utterly baseless and lying slander has ever been invented and circulated. “Silly people can see but silly dreams,” says a Russian proverb. It makes one’s blood boil to hear such vile accusations made without the slightest foundation, and on the strength of mere inferences. Ask the hundreds of honorable English men and women who have been members of the Theosophical Society for years whether an *immoral* precept or a *harmful* doctrine was ever taught to them. Open the *Secret Doctrine*, and you will find page after page denouncing the Jews and other nations precisely on account of this devotion to Phallic rites, due to the literal interpretation of nature symbolism, and the grossly materialistic conceptions of her dualism in all the *exoteric* creeds. Such ceaseless and malicious misrepresentation of our teachings and beliefs is really disgraceful.

<term>THEO.</term> Nor do I deny it; only I maintain that this proves no more than does its presence in Christianity, the religion of the West. Read Hargrave Jennings’ *Rosicrucians*, if you would

assure yourself of it. In the East, phallic symbolism is, perhaps, cruder because it is truer to nature—or, I would rather say, more *naive* and sincere than in the West. But it is not more licentious, nor does it suggest the same gross and coarse ideas to the Oriental mind as it does to the Western mind, with perhaps one or two exceptions, such as the shameful sect known as the "Maharajah," or *Vallabhacharya* sect.

<term>ENQ.</term> A writer in the *Agnostic Journal*—one of your accusers—has just hinted that the followers of this disgraceful sect are Theosophists and "claim true Theosophic insight."

<term>THEO.</term> He wrote a falsehood, and that is all. There never was, nor is there at present, one single Vallabhacharya in our Society. As for their having or claiming Theosophic insight, that is another lie based on crass ignorance about Indian sects. Their "Maharajah" only claims a right to the money, wives, and daughters of his foolish followers and nothing more. This sect is despised by all the other Hindus.

But you will find the whole subject dealt with at length in the *Secret Doctrine*, to which I must again refer you for detailed explanations. To conclude, the very soul of Theosophy is dead set against phallic worship; and its occult or esoteric section is even more opposed to it than the exoteric teachings. There never was a more lying statement made than the one above. And now, ask me some other questions.

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

### IS THE THEOSOPHICAL SOCIETY A MONEY-MAKING CONCERN?

**ENQ.** Agreed. Well, have either of the Founders, Colonel H. S. Olcott or H. P. Blavatsky, ever made any money, profit, or derived any worldly benefit from the T.S., as some newspapers claim?

**THEO.** Not one penny. The newspapers lie. On the contrary, they have both given all they had, and literally impoverished themselves. As for "worldly benefits," think of the slander and vilification they have been subjected to, and then ask that question!

**ENQ.** Yet I have read in many missionary publications that the entrance fees and subscriptions more than covered all expenses; and one said that the Founders were making twenty thousand pounds a year!

**THEO.** This is a falsehood, like many others. In the published accounts of January 1889, you will find an exact statement of all the money ever received from any source since 1879. The total received from all sources (entrance fees, donations, etc.) during these ten years is under six thousand pounds, and of this, a large part was contributed by the Founders themselves from the proceeds of their private resources and their literary work. All this has been openly and officially admitted, even by our enemies, the Society for Psychical Research <note>The text refers to them as the "Psychic Research Society."</note>. And now both the Founders are penniless: one, too old and ill to work as she did before, unable to spare time for outside literary work to help the Society financially, can only write for the Theosophical cause; the other keeps laboring for it as before, and receives just as little thanks for it.

<summary>The author refutes claims that the Founders of the Theosophical Society profited financially, asserting that they actually exhausted their personal resources and received very little in total donations over ten years.</summary>
<keywords>Theosophical Society, finances, H. S. Olcott, H. P. Blavatsky, Society for Psychical Research, donations, missionary publications</keywords>

--- Page 298 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist regarding the financial status of the Society’s Founders, specifically addressing why they do not pursue external professional income.</meta>

<page-num>282</page-num>
->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

ENQ.  But surely they need money to live?

THEO.  Not at all. As long as they have food and lodging, even though they owe it to the devotion of a few friends, they need little more.

ENQ.  But couldn't Madame Blavatsky, especially, make more than enough to live on from her writings?

THEO.  When she was in India, she received an average of about a thousand rupees a year for articles contributed to Russian and other newspapers, but she gave it all away to the Society.

ENQ.  Political articles?

THEO.  Never. Everything she has written throughout her seven-year stay in India is available in print. It deals only with the religions, ethnology, and customs of India, and with Theosophy—never with politics, about which she knows nothing and cares even less. Again, two years ago she refused several contracts totaling about 1,200 gold rubles per month; she could not accept them without abandoning her work for the Society, which required all her time and strength. She has documents to prove it.

ENQ.  But why couldn't both she and Colonel Olcott do as others—notably many Theosophists—do: follow their respective professions and devote their spare time to the work of the Society?

THEO.  Because by serving two masters, either the professional or the philanthropic work would have had to suffer. Every true Theosophist is morally bound to sacrifice the personal for the impersonal, his own present good for the future benefit of other people. If the Founders do not set the example, who will?

<summary>The Theosophist explains that the Founders live simply and sacrifice personal wealth for the Society, noting that Madame Blavatsky donated her literary earnings and refused high-paying contracts to focus on her work. The text emphasizes that true Theosophists must prioritize philanthropic duty over personal professional gain.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Enquirer and the Theosophist, moving from the personal sacrifices of the Founders to the financial state of the Theosophical Society as an organization.</meta>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

ENQ. And are there many who follow it?

THEO. I am bound to tell you the truth. In Europe, there are about half a dozen in all, out of more than that many branches.

ENQ. Then it is not true that the Theosophical Society has a large capital or endowment of its own?

THEO. That is false, for it has none at all. Now that the entrance fee of £1 and the small annual dues have been abolished, it is even a doubtful question whether the staff at the headquarters in India will not soon starve to death.

ENQ. Then why not raise subscriptions?

THEO. We are not the Salvation Army; we cannot and *have never* begged; nor have we ever followed the example of the churches and sects by "taking up collections." The money occasionally sent for the support of the Society—the small sums contributed by some devoted <term>Fellows</term>—consists entirely of voluntary donations.

ENQ. But I have heard of large sums of money being given to Madame Blavatsky. It was said four years ago that she received £5,000 from one rich young "Fellow" who went out to join them in India, and £10,000 from another wealthy and well-known American gentleman—one of your members who died in Europe four years ago.

THEO. Tell those who told you this that they are either originating or repeating a gross falsehood. "Madame Blavatsky" has never asked for or received ONE PENNY from the two gentlemen mentioned above, nor anything close to that amount from anyone else, since the Theosophical Society was founded. Let any living person try to prove this slander, and it would be easier for him to prove that the Bank of England is bankrupt than

<meta>This page continues the refutation of financial slanders against Madame Blavatsky and the Theosophical Society, specifically addressing rumors regarding large legacies.</meta>

<page-num>284</page-num>
-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...that the said “Founder” has ever made any money out of Theosophy. These two slanders were started by two high-born ladies belonging to the London aristocracy and were immediately traced and disproved. They are the dead bodies, the carcasses of two fabrications which, after having been buried in the sea of oblivion, are once more raised to the surface of the stagnant waters of slander.

ENQ. Then I have been told of several large legacies left to the <term>T.S.</term> <note>Theosophical Society</note>. One—about £8,000—was left to it by some eccentric Englishman who did not even belong to the Society. The other—£3,000 or £4,000—was willed by an Australian <term>F.T.S.</term> <note>Fellow of the Theosophical Society</note>. Is this true?

THEO. I heard of the first; and I also know that, whether legally left or not, the T.S. has never profited from it, nor have the Founders ever been officially notified of it. Because our Society was not then a chartered body, and thus had no legal existence, the Judge at the Court of Probate, as we were told, paid no attention to such a legacy and turned the sum over to the heirs. So much for the first. As for the second, it is quite true. The testator was one of our devoted Fellows and willed all he had to the T.S. But when the President, Colonel Olcott, came to look into the matter, he found that the testator had children whom he had disinherited for some family reasons. Therefore, he called a council, and it was decided that the legacy should be refused and the money passed to the legal heirs. The Theosophical Society would be untrue to its name were it to profit from money to which others are essentially entitled—at any rate on Theosophical principles—if not legally.

<summary>The Theosophist refutes rumors of the Society's wealth by explaining that a large English legacy was never received due to the Society's lack of legal status, while another Australian legacy was voluntarily refused to ensure the testator's disinherited children received their rightful inheritance.</summary>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

ENQ. Again—and I say this on the authority of your own journal, the *Theosophist*—there is a Rajah of India who donated 25,000 rupees to the Society. Did you not thank him for his great generosity in the January *Theosophist* for 1888?

THEO. We did, in these words: "That the thanks of the Convention be conveyed to His Highness the Maharajah . . . for his promised generous gift of 25,000 rupees to the Society's Fund." The thanks were duly conveyed, but the money is still only a "promise" and has never reached the Headquarters.

ENQ. But surely, if the Maharajah promised and was thanked for his gift publicly and in print, he will keep his promise?

THEO. He might, though the promise is 18 months old. I am speaking of the present and not of the future.

ENQ. Then how do you propose to continue?

THEO. As long as the <term>T.S.</term> <note>Theosophical Society</note> has a few devoted members willing to work for it without reward or thanks, and as long as a few good Theosophists support it with occasional donations, it will exist, and nothing can crush it.

ENQ. I have heard many Theosophists speak of a "power behind the Society" and of certain <term>Mahatmas</term> <note>Great Souls or Masters</note>—also mentioned in Mr. Sinnett's works—who are said to have founded the Society and to watch over and protect it.

THEO. You may laugh, but it is true.

->*THE WORKING STAFF OF THE <term>T.S.</term>*<-

ENQ. I have heard that these men are great Adepts, Alchemists, and so forth. If, then, they can change lead into gold and make as much

--- Page 302 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between an Inquirer and a Theosophist regarding the alleged supernatural powers of the Society's hidden Masters and why those powers are not used to solve the Society's financial difficulties.</meta>

<page-num>286</page-num>

-># THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

money as they like, besides performing all kinds of miracles at will, as related in Mr. Sinnett’s *Occult World*, why do they not provide you with money and support the Founders and the Society in comfort?

THEO. Because they did not found a “miracle club.” Because the Society is intended to help people develop the powers latent within them through their own efforts and merit. Because whatever they may or may not produce in the way of phenomena, they are not *counterfeiters*; nor would they place an additional and very strong temptation in the path of members and candidates: *Theosophy is not to be bought*. Until now, for the past 14 years, not a single working member has ever received pay or a salary from either the Masters or the Society.

ENQ. Then are none of your workers paid at all?

THEO. Until now, not one. But as everyone has to eat, drink, and clothe themselves, all those who are without any means of their own and devote their whole time to the work of the Society are provided with the necessities of life at the Headquarters in Madras, India, though these “necessities” are humble enough, in truth! (See Rules at the end.) But now that the Society’s work has increased so greatly and continues to increase (N.B., *owing to slanders*) in Europe, we need more working hands. We hope to have a few members who will hereafter be remunerated—if the word *can* be used in these cases. For every one of these <term>Fellows</term> <note>Members of the Theosophical Society</note>, who are preparing to give *all* their time to the Society, is leaving a good official position with excellent prospects to work for us at *less than half of their former salary*.

ENQ. And who will provide the funds for this?

THEO. Some of our Fellows who are just a little richer than the rest. Anyone who would speculate or make money from Theosophy would be unworthy to remain in our ranks.

ENQ. But you must surely make money from your books, magazines, and other publications?

THEO. The *Theosophist* of Madras, alone among the magazines, generates a profit, and this has regularly been turned over to the Society, year by year, as the published accounts show. *Lucifer* is slowly but steadily engulfing money, having never yet paid its expenses—thanks to being boycotted by the pious booksellers and railway stalls. *The Lotus* in France—started with the private and modest funds of a Theosophist who devoted his whole time and labor to it—has ceased to exist, alas, due to the same causes! Nor does the *New York Path* pay its own way, while the *Revue Théosophique* of Paris has only just been started, also from the private funds of a lady member. Moreover, whenever any of the works issued by the Theosophical Publishing Company in London *do* make a profit, the proceeds will be devoted to the service of the Society.

ENQ. And now please tell me all you can about the Mahatmas. So many absurd and contradictory things are said about them that one does not know what to believe, and all sorts of ridiculous stories are circulating.

THEO. You may well call them "ridiculous!"

<summary>The Theosophist explains that the Society's publications are mostly funded privately and do not generate profit, with any surplus being donated back to the Society. The conversation then shifts to clarifying the nature of the Mahatmas due to widespread rumors.</summary>

XIV.

# THE "THEOSOPHICAL MAHATMAS."

---

## *ARE THEY "SPIRITS OF LIGHT" OR "GOBLINS DAMNED"?*

ENQ. Finally, who are those you call your "Masters"? Some say they are "Spirits," or some other kind of supernatural beings, while others call them "myths."

THEO. They are neither. I once heard an outsider say to another that they were a sort of *male mermaids*, whatever such a creature may be. But if you listen to what people say, you will never have a true understanding of them. In the first place, they are *living men*, born just as we are born, and destined to die like every other mortal.

ENQ. Yes, but it is rumored that some of them are a thousand years old. Is this true?

THEO. It is as true as the miraculous growth of hair on the head of Meredith's Shagpat. <note>A reference to George Meredith's 1856 fantasy novel, *The Shaving of Shagpat*.</note> Truly, like the "Identical" hair in that story, no Theosophical shaving has been able to trim it until now. The more we deny these stories, and the more we try to correct people, the more absurd the fabrications become. I have heard of Methuselah being 969 years old; but, not being forced to believe in it,

<summary>The Theosophist clarifies that the "Masters" or Mahatmas are not supernatural spirits or myths, but living men who are subject to mortality. They dismiss rumors about the Mahatmas' extreme longevity as absurd fabrications, comparing them to literary fantasies.</summary>

<keywords>Theosophical Mahatmas, Masters, supernatural beings, living men, Meredith's Shagpat, Methuselah, longevity myths</keywords>

--- Page 305 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue regarding the nature and longevity of the "Masters," specifically defining the term "Mahatma" and explaining why these individuals live apart from modern society.</meta>

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

...have laughed at the statement, for which I was immediately regarded by many as a blasphemous heretic.

ENQ. Seriously, though, do they outlive the ordinary age of men?

THEO. What do you call the ordinary age? I remember reading in the *Lancet* of a Mexican who was almost 190 years old; but I have never heard of a mortal man, layman, or Adept, who could live even half the years allotted to Methuselah. Some Adepts do exceed, by a good deal, what you would call the ordinary age; yet there is nothing miraculous in it, and very few of them care to live very long.

ENQ. But what does the word “Mahatma” really mean?

THEO. Simply a “great soul,” great through moral elevation and intellectual attainment. If the title of "great" is given to a drunken soldier like Alexander, why should we not call those “Great” who have achieved far greater conquests in nature’s secrets than Alexander ever did on the field of battle? Besides, the term is an Indian and a very old word.

ENQ. And why do you call them “Masters”?

THEO. We call them “Masters” because they are our teachers; and because from them we have derived all the Theosophical truths, however inadequately some of us may have expressed them, and others understood them. They are men of great learning, whom we term Initiates, and of still greater holiness of life. They are not ascetics in the ordinary sense, though they certainly remain apart from the turmoil and strife of your Western world.

ENQ. But is it not selfish to isolate themselves this way?

THEO. Where is the selfishness? Does not the fate of the Theoso-

In their own country, they move about as publicly as other people do.

**Enq.** Don't you attribute supernatural powers to them?

**Theo.** We believe in nothing supernatural, as I have told you already. Had Edison lived and invented his phonograph two hundred years ago, he would most likely have been burned along with it, and the whole thing attributed to the devil. The powers which they exercise are simply the development of potencies lying latent in every man and woman, and the existence of which even official science is beginning to recognize.

**Enq.** Is it true that these men inspire some of your writers, and that many, if not all, of your Theosophical works were written under their dictation?

**Theo.** Some have been. There are passages entirely dictated by them and *verbatim*, but in most cases they only inspire the ideas and leave the literary form to the writers.

**Enq.** But this in itself is miraculous; it is, in fact, a miracle. How can they do it?

**Theo.** My dear Sir, you are laboring under a great mistake, and science itself will refute your arguments in the near future. Why should it be a "miracle," as you call it? A miracle is supposed to mean some operation which is supernatural, whereas there is really nothing above or beyond Nature and Nature's laws. Among the many forms of the "miracle"

which have come under modern scientific recognition, there is Hypnotism, and one phase of its power is known as “Suggestion,” a form of thought transference which has been successfully used in combating particular physical diseases, etc. The time is not far distant when the world of science will be forced to acknowledge that there exists as much interaction between one mind and another, no matter at what distance, as between one body and another in the closest contact. When two minds are sympathetically related, and the instruments through which they function are tuned to respond magnetically and electrically to one another, there is nothing which will prevent the transmission of thoughts from one to the other at will; for since the mind is not of a tangible nature such that distance can divide it from the subject of its contemplation, it follows that the only difference that can exist between two minds is a difference of STATE. So if this latter hindrance is overcome, where is the “*miracle*” of thought transference, at whatever distance?

**Enq.** But you will admit that Hypnotism does nothing so miraculous or wonderful as that?

**Theo.** On the contrary, it is a well-established fact that a hypnotist can affect the brain of his subject so far as to produce an expression of his own thoughts, and even his words, through the organism of his subject; and although the phenomena attaching to this method of actual thought transference are as yet few in number, no one, I presume, will undertake to say how far their action may extend in the future, when the laws that govern their production are more scientifically

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

**ENQ.** Then why do our physicians not experiment and see if they could do as much? <note>Such, for instance, as Professor Bernheim and Dr. C. Lloyd Tuckey of England; Professors Beaunis and Liégeois of Nancy; Delbœuf of Liège; Burot and Bourru of Rochefort; Fontan and Sigard of Bordeaux; Forel of Zurich; and Doctors Despine of Marseilles; Van Renterghem and Van Eeden of Amsterdam; Wetterstrand of Stockholm; Schrenck-Notzing of Leipzig; and many other eminent physicians and writers.</note>

**THEO.** Because, first of all, they are not Adepts with a thorough understanding of the secrets and laws of the psychic and spiritual realms, but materialists, afraid to step outside the narrow groove of matter; and, secondly, because they must fail for now, and indeed until they are led to acknowledge that such powers are attainable.

**ENQ.** And could they be taught?

**THEO.** Not unless they were first prepared by having the materialistic dross they have accumulated in their brains swept away to the very last atom.

**ENQ.** This is very interesting. Tell me, have the Adepts inspired or dictated to many of your Theosophists in this way?

**THEO.** No; on the contrary, to very few. Such operations require special conditions. An unscrupulous but skilled Adept of the Black Brotherhood (whom we call “Brothers of the Shadow” and <term>Dugpas</term>) has far fewer difficulties to work under. For,

->THE KEY TO THEOSOPHY.<-

having no spiritual laws to restrain his actions, such a <term>Dugpa</term> “sorcerer” will unceremoniously obtain control over any mind and subject it entirely to his evil powers. But our Masters will never do that. They have no right—except by falling into Black Magic—to obtain full mastery over anyone’s immortal Ego, and can therefore act only on the physical and psychic nature of the subject, thereby leaving the person’s free will completely undisturbed. Hence, unless a person has been brought into a psychic relationship with the Masters and is assisted by virtue of his full faith in and devotion to his Teachers, the latter, whenever transmitting their thoughts to someone for whom these conditions are not fulfilled, experience great difficulties in penetrating the cloudy chaos of that person’s sphere. But this is not the place to discuss a subject of this nature. It is enough to say that if the power exists, then there are Intelligences (embodied or disembodied) which guide this power, and living conscious instruments through whom it is transmitted and by whom it is received. We have only to beware of *black magic*.

**ENQ.** But what do you really mean by “black magic”?

**THEO.** Simply the abuse of psychic powers or of any secret of nature; the act of applying the powers of Occultism to selfish and sinful ends. A hypnotist who, taking advantage of his powers of “suggestion,” forces a subject to steal or murder, would be called a black magician by us. The famous “rejuvenation system” of Dr. Brown-Séquard of Paris, involving a loathsome animal injection into human blood—a discovery all the medical journals of Europe are now discussing—is, if true, *unconscious black magic*.

**ENQ.** But this is a medieval belief in witchcraft and sorcery! Even the Law itself has ceased to believe in such things?

**THEO.** So much the worse for the law, as it has been led, through such a lack of discrimination, into committing more than one judicial mistake and crime. It is the term alone that frightens you with its "superstitious" ring. Would not the law punish an abuse of hypnotic powers, as I just mentioned? Indeed, it has already punished it in France and Germany; yet it would indignantly deny that it was applying punishment to a crime of evident sorcery. You cannot believe in the effectiveness and reality of the *powers of suggestion by physicians and mesmerists* (or hypnotists), and then refuse to believe in the same powers when used for evil motives. And if you do, then you believe in *Sorcery*. You cannot believe in good and disbelieve in evil, or accept genuine money and refuse to believe in the existence of counterfeit coins. Nothing can exist without its contrast, and no day, no light, and no good could be represented as such in your consciousness if there were no night, darkness, or evil to offset and contrast them.

**ENQ.** Indeed, I have known men who, while thoroughly believing in what you call great psychic or magic powers, laughed at the very mention of Witchcraft and Sorcery.

**THEO.** What does it prove? Simply that they are illogical. So much the worse for them, again. And we, knowing as we do of the existence of good and holy Adepts, believe just as thoroughly in the existence of bad and unholy Adepts, or—*<term>Dugpas</term>*.

**ENQ.** But if the Masters exist, why don't they come out before everyone?

and refute once and for all the many charges made against Madame Blavatsky and the Society?

**THEO.** What charges?

**ENQ.** That they do not exist, and that she has invented them. That they are men of straw, "Mahatmas of muslin and bladders." Does not all this injure her reputation?

**THEO.** In what way can such an accusation really injure her? Did she ever make money from their presumed existence, or derive benefit or fame from it? I answer that she has gained only insults, abuse, and slanders, which would have been very painful if she had not learned long ago to remain perfectly indifferent to such false charges. For what does it amount to, after all? Why, it amounts to an implied compliment, which—if those fools, her accusers, were not carried away by their blind hatred—they would have thought twice before uttering. To say that she has invented the Masters comes to this: she must have invented every bit of philosophy that has ever been published in Theosophical literature. She must be the author of the letters from which "Esoteric Buddhism" was written; the sole inventor of every tenet found in the "Secret Doctrine," which, if the world were just, would be recognized as supplying many of the missing links of science, as will be discovered a hundred years from now. By saying what they do, they are also giving her the credit of being far cleverer than the hundreds of men (many *very* clever and quite a few scientific men) who believe in what she says—since she must have fooled them all! If they speak the truth, then she must be several Mahatmas rolled into one like a set of Chinese nesting boxes;

since among the so-called “<term>Mahatma letters</term>” are many in totally different and distinct styles, all of which her accusers declare that she has written.

**ENQ.** It is just what they say. But is it not very painful to her to be publicly denounced as “the most accomplished impostor of the age, whose name deserves to pass to posterity,” as is done in the Report of the “<term>Society for Psychical Research</term>”?

**THEO.** It might be painful if it were true, or came from people less rabidly materialistic and prejudiced. As it is, personally she treats the whole matter with contempt, while the <term>Mahatmas</term> simply laugh at it. In truth, it is the greatest compliment that could be paid to her. I say so, again.

**ENQ.** But her enemies claim to have proved their case.

**THEO.** Yes, it is easy enough to make such a claim when you have appointed yourself judge, jury, and prosecuting counsel all at once, as they did. But who, except their direct followers and our enemies, believes in it?

**ENQ.** But they sent a representative to <term>India</term> to investigate the matter, didn’t they?

**THEO.** They did, and their final conclusion rests entirely on the unchecked statements and unverified assertions of this young gentleman. A lawyer who read through his report told a friend of mine that in all his experience he had never seen “such a ridiculous and self-condemnatory document.” It was found to be full of suppositions and “working hypotheses” which mutually destroyed each other. Is this a serious charge?

**ENQ.** Yet it has done the Society great harm. Why, then, did she not vindicate her own character, at least, before a <term>Court of Law</term>?

**THEO.** Firstly, because as a Theosophist, it is her duty to ignore all personal insults. Secondly, because neither the Society nor Madame Blavatsky had any money to waste on such a lawsuit. And when you remember that they would have already been turned against us by the “Russian Spy” scare, the charge of *atheism and infidelity*, and all the other slanders that have been circulated against us, you cannot fail to see that such an attempt to obtain justice in a court of law would have been worse than fruitless! All this the Psychic Researchers knew well, and they took a vile and petty advantage of their position to elevate themselves and save their reputations at our expense.

This is amply proven by the fact that no mistakes have been able to cripple it, and no scandals from within, nor the most damaging attacks from without, have been able to overthrow it. The <term>Masters</term> look at the future, not at the present, and every mistake is simply more accumulated wisdom for the days to come. That other “Master” who sent the man with the five talents did not tell him how to double them, nor did he prevent the foolish servant from burying his one talent in the earth. Each person must acquire wisdom through their own experience and merits. The Christian Churches, who claim a far higher “Master”—the very Holy Ghost itself—have always been and are still guilty not only of “mistakes,” but of a series of bloody crimes throughout the ages. ### THE ABUSE OF SACRED NAMES AND TERMS.

**ENQ.** Then, is what I have heard—namely, that many of your Theosophical writers claim to have been inspired by these <term>Masters</term>, or to have seen and conversed with them—not true?

**THEO.** It may or it may not be true. How can I tell? The burden of proof rests with them. Some of them, a few—very few, indeed—have distinctly either lied or were hallucinated when boasting of such inspiration; others were truly inspired by great <term>Adepts</term>. The tree is known by its fruits; and as all Theosophists have to be judged by their deeds and not by what they write or say, so *all* Theosophical books must be accepted on their merits, and not according to any claim to authority which they may put forward.

**ENQ.** But would Madame Blavatsky apply this to her own works—the *Secret Doctrine*, for instance?

**THEO.** Certainly; she says expressly in the PREFACE that she gives out the doctrines that she has learned from the <term>Masters</term>, but claims no inspiration whatever for what she has lately written. As for our best Theosophists, they would also in this case far rather that the names of the <term>Masters</term> had never been mixed up with our books in any way. With few exceptions, most of such works are not only imperfect, but positively erroneous and

<summary>The author argues that Theosophical writings should be judged on their own merits rather than claims of inspiration from Masters, noting that even Madame Blavatsky's work makes no claim to divine inspiration. It is acknowledged that some claims of such inspiration are false or based on delusion.</summary>
<keywords>Mahatmas, Holy Ghost, Adepts, Theosophists, Mdme. Blavatsky, Secret Doctrine, Masters, inspiration, authority, ecclesiastical history</keywords>

misleading. Great are the desecrations to which the names of two of the <term>Masters</term> have been subjected. There is hardly a <term>medium</term> who has not claimed to have seen them. Every bogus, swindling society, for commercial purposes, now claims to be guided and directed by “<term>Masters</term>,” often supposed to be far higher than ours! Many and heavy are the sins of those who advanced these claims, prompted either by a desire for profit, vanity, or irresponsible <term>mediumship</term>. # CONCLUSION.

### THE FUTURE OF THE THEOSOPHICAL SOCIETY.

ENQUIRER. Tell me, what do you expect for Theosophy in the future?

THEOSOPHIST. If you speak of THEOSOPHY, I answer that, as it has existed eternally throughout the endless cycles upon cycles of the Past, so it will ever exist throughout the infinitudes of the Future, because Theosophy is synonymous with EVERLASTING TRUTH.

ENQUIRER. Pardon me; I meant to ask you instead about the prospects of the Theosophical Society.

THEOSOPHIST. Its future will depend almost entirely upon the degree of selflessness, earnestness, devotion, and last, but not least, on the amount of knowledge and wisdom possessed by those members who will be responsible for carrying on the work and directing the Society after the death of the Founders.

ENQUIRER. I quite see the importance of their being selfless and devoted, but I do not quite grasp how their *knowledge* can be as vital a factor in the question as these other qualities. Surely the literature which already exists, and to which constant additions are still being made, ought to be sufficient?

THEOSOPHIST. I do not refer to technical knowledge of the esoteric doctrine, though that is most important; I spoke rather of the great

the need which our successors in the guidance of the Society will have for unbiased and clear judgment. Every attempt like the Theosophical Society has so far ended in failure because, sooner or later, it has degenerated into a sect, established its own rigid dogmas, and thus gradually lost that vitality which only living truth can provide. You must remember that all our members were born and raised in some creed or religion, and that all are more or less products of their generation both physically and mentally. Consequently, their judgment is only too likely to be warped and unconsciously biased by some or all of these influences. If, then, they cannot be freed from such inherent bias, or at least taught to recognize it instantly and so avoid being led away by it, the result can only be that the Society will drift onto some sandbank of thought or another and remain there as a stranded carcass to decay and die.

ENQUIRER. But if this danger is averted?

THEOSOPHIST. Then the Society will live on into and through the twentieth century. It will gradually influence and permeate the great mass of thinking and intelligent people with its broad-minded and noble ideas of Religion, Duty, and Philanthropy. Slowly but surely, it will burst apart the iron chains of creeds and dogmas, and of social and caste prejudices; it will break down racial and national hostilities and barriers, and will open the way to the practical realization of the Brotherhood of all men. Through its teaching, through the philosophy which it has made accessible and understandable to the modern mind, the West will learn to understand and appreciate the East at its

# GLOSSARY.

## A.

**Absoluteness.** When applied to the UNIVERSAL PRINCIPLE, it denotes an abstraction, which is more accurate and logical than applying the adjective "absolute" to that which can have neither attributes nor limitations.

**Adam Kadmon** (*Heb.*) "Archetypal man, Humanity. The 'Heavenly man' who has not fallen into sin. Kabbalists refer it to the Ten Sephiroth on the level of human perception." In the *Kabala*, Adam Kadmon is the manifested Logos corresponding to our *third* Logos, while the unmanifested is the first paradigmatic *ideal* man, symbolizing the universe in *abscondito*, or in its "privation" in the Aristotelian sense.

The first Logos is "the light of the World," while the second and the third are its gradually deepening shadows.

**Adept** (*Lat. adeptus*). In Occultism, one who has reached the stage of initiation and become a master in the Science of Esoteric Philosophy.

**Æther** (*Gr.*) For the Ancients, the Divine luminiferous substance which pervades the whole universe; the "garment" of the Supreme Deity, Zeus, or Jupiter. For the Moderns, Ether; for the meaning of which, in physics and chemistry, see Webster's Dictionary, or some

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

other. In Esotericism, <term>Æther</term> is the third principle of the Cosmic Septenary, matter (earth) being the lowest, and <term>Akâsa</term>, the highest.

**Agathon** (*Gr.*) Plato's Supreme Deity, literally "the good." Our <term>ALAYA</term> or the Soul of the World.

**Agnostic.** A word first used by Professor Huxley to indicate one who believes nothing that cannot be demonstrated by the senses.

**Ahankara** (*Sans.*) The conception of "I," self-consciousness or self-identity; the "I," or egoistical and *mayavic* principle in man, due to our ignorance which separates our "I" from the Universal <term>ONE</term>-Self. Personality, egoism also.

**Ain-Soph** (*Heb.*) The "Boundless" or "Limitless" Deity emanating and extending. <term>Ain-Soph</term> is also written En-Soph and Ain-Suph, for no one, not even the Rabbis, is quite sure of their vowels. In the religious metaphysics of the old Hebrew philosophers, the <term>ONE</term> Principle was an abstraction like <term>Parabrahm</term>, though modern Kabbalists have succeeded by mere force of sophistry and paradoxes in making a "Supreme God" of it, and nothing higher. But with the early Chaldean Kabbalists, Ain-Soph was "without form or being" with "no likeness to anything else." (Franck's *Die Kabbala*, p. 126.) That Ain-Soph has never been considered as the "Creator" is proved conclusively by the fact that such an orthodox Jew as Philo calls "creator" the Logos, who stands next to the "Limitless One," and is "the <term>SECOND</term> God." "The <term>SECOND</term> God is in its (Ain-Soph's) wisdom," says Philo in *Quæst et Solut.* Deity is no-thing; it is nameless, and therefore called Ain-Soph—the word *Ain* meaning nothing. (See also Franck's *Kabbala*, p. 153.)

**Alchemy**, in Arabic Ul-Khemi, is as the name suggests, the chemistry of nature. <term>Ul-Khemi</term> or <term>Al-Kimia</term>, however, is really an Arabianized word, taken from the Greek <term>χημεία</term> from <term>χυμός</term> "juice," extracted from a plant. Alchemy deals with the finer forces of nature and the various conditions of matter in which they are found to operate. Seeking under the veil of language, more or less artificial, to convey to

<meta>This page continues the definition of Alchemy, explaining its core principles, historical origins, and the different levels of its practice—from the material to the spiritual.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

the uninitiated as much of the *Mysterium Magnum* as is safe in the hands of a selfish world, the <term>Alchemist</term> postulates as his first principle the existence of a certain <term>Universal Solvent</term> in the homogeneous substance from which the elements were evolved; this substance he calls pure gold, or *summum materiæ*. This solvent, also called *menstruum universale*, possesses the power to remove all seeds of disease from the human body, to renew youth, and to prolong life. Such is the *lapis philosophorum* (the philosopher's stone). <term>Alchemy</term> first penetrated Europe through <term>Geber</term>, the great Arabian sage and philosopher, in the eighth century of our era; but it was known and practiced long ages ago in China and Egypt. Numerous papyri on alchemy, and other proofs that it was the favorite study of kings and priests, have been exhumed and preserved under the generic name of <term>Hermetic</term> treatises (see *Tabula Smaragdina*). Alchemy is studied under three distinct aspects, which allow for many different interpretations: the Cosmic, the Human, and the Terrestrial.

These three methods were represented by the three alchemical properties—sulphur, mercury, and salt. Different writers have stated that these consist of three, seven, ten, and twelve processes respectively; but they all agree there is only one object in alchemy, which is to transmute base metals into pure gold. But what that *gold* really is, very few people understand correctly. No doubt there is such a thing in nature as the transmutation of base metal into noble metal; but this is only one aspect of alchemy, the terrestrial or purely material, for we logically see the same process taking place within the earth. Yet, beyond this interpretation, there is a symbolic meaning in alchemy that is purely psychic and spiritual. While the <term>Kabalist-Alchemist</term> seeks the realization of the former, the <term>Occultist-Alchemist</term>, rejecting the gold of the earth, directs all his attention and efforts only toward the transmutation of the base quaternary into the divine upper trinity of man, which, when finally blended, is one. The spiritual, mental,

<meta>This page continues the discussion of alchemy, explaining its relation to the four elements and its historical precedence over known mythologies and chemistry, before beginning an entry on the Alexandrian Philosophers.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

The psychic and physical planes of human existence are compared in <term>Alchemy</term> to the four elements—fire, air, water, and earth—and each is capable of a three-fold constitution, *that is*, fixed, unstable, and volatile. Little or nothing is known by the world concerning the origin of this archaic branch of philosophy; but it is certain that it predates the construction of any known Zodiac and, as it deals with the personified forces of nature, it probably also predates any of the mythologies of the world. Nor is there any doubt that the true secrets of <term>transmutation</term> (on the physical plane) were known in ancient times and lost before the dawn of the so-called historical period. Modern chemistry owes its best fundamental discoveries to <term>Alchemy</term>, but regardless of the undeniable truth of the latter—that there is only *one* element in the universe—chemistry placed metals in the category of elements and is only now beginning to find out its major mistake. Even some encyclopedists are forced to confess that if most of the accounts of transmutation are fraud or delusion,

> “yet some of them are accompanied by testimony which makes them seem probable. By means of the galvanic battery, even the alkalies have been discovered to have a metallic basis. The possibility of obtaining metal from other substances which contain the ingredients composing it, or of changing one metal into another . . . must therefore be left undecided. Nor are all Alchemists to be considered impostors. Many have labored with the conviction of achieving their goal with tireless patience and purity of heart, which is strongly recommended by Alchemists as the primary requirement for the success of their labors.” (Popular Encyclopedia)

**Alexandrian Philosophers** (or School). This famous school arose in <term>Alexandria</term>, Egypt, which was for many ages the center of learning and philosophy. It was famous for its library, founded by <term>Ptolemy Soter</term> at the very beginning of his reign (Ptolemy died in 283 B.C.)—a library which once boasted 700,000 scrolls, or volumes (<term>Aulus Gellius</term>)—for its museum, the first real Academy of Sciences and Arts, and for its

--- Page 329 ---
<meta>This page continues the glossary, focusing on the historical importance of Alexandria as a center of learning and defining key philosophical terms like altruism and the schools associated with Ammonius Saccas.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

Alexandria was home to world-renowned scholars, such as Euclid, the father of scientific geometry; Apollonius of Perga, the author of the still existing work on conic sections; and Nicomachus, the mathematician. It also hosted astronomers, natural philosophers, and anatomists such as Herophilus and Erasistratus, as well as physicians, musicians, artists, and others. But it became even more famous for its eclectic or *new* Platonic school, founded by Ammonius Saccas in 173 A.D., whose disciples included Origen, Plotinus, and many other men now famous in history. The most celebrated schools of the Gnostics had their origin in Alexandria. Philo Judaeus, Josephus, Iamblichus, Porphyry, Clement of Alexandria, Eratosthenes the astronomer, Hypatia the virgin philosopher, and countless other secondary stars all belonged to these great schools at various times. Together, they helped make Alexandria one of the most justly renowned seats of learning that the world has ever produced.

**Altruism**, from *Alter*, meaning other. A quality opposed to egoism. It refers to actions that tend to do good for others, regardless of oneself.

**Ammonius Saccas.** A great and good philosopher who lived in Alexandria between the 2nd and 3rd centuries of our era. He was the founder of the Neo-Platonic School of the Philalethians, or "lovers of truth." He was of humble birth and born to Christian parents, but he was endowed with such prominent, almost divine goodness that he was called *Theodidaktos*, or the "God-taught." He honored what was good in Christianity but broke with it and the churches at an early age, being unable to find any superiority in Christianity over the old religions.

**Analogeticists.** The disciples of Ammonius Saccas (*see above*), so called because of their practice of interpreting all sacred legends, myths, and mysteries through a principle of analogy and correspondence. This rule is now found in the Kabbalistic system, and most prominently in the schools of Esoteric philosophy in the East. (See "The Twelve Signs of the Zodiac" by T. Subba Row in *Five Years of Theosophy*.)

Subba Row</vocab>

--- Page 330 ---
thought<meta>This page continues the glossary, defining terms from Ananda to Anima Mundi, including a detailed biography of the philosopher Anaxagoras and his scientific and metaphysical theories.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

**Ananda** (*Sans*.) Bliss, joy, felicity, happiness. The name of a favorite disciple of Gautama, the Lord Buddha.

**Anaxagoras.** A famous Ionian philosopher who lived around 500 B.C. He studied philosophy under Anaximenes of Miletus and settled in Athens during the era of Pericles. Socrates, Euripides, Archelaus, and other distinguished men and philosophers were among his disciples and pupils. He was a highly learned astronomer and was one of the first to openly explain what Pythagoras had taught in secret—namely, the movements of the planets, the eclipses of the sun and moon, and so on. It was he who taught the theory of chaos, based on the principle that “nothing comes from nothing” (*ex nihilo nihil fit*), and the theory of atoms as the underlying essence and substance of all bodies, being “of the same nature as the bodies they formed.” He taught that these atoms were primarily set in motion by *nous* (universal intelligence, or the *Mahat* of the Hindus), and that this *nous* is an immaterial, eternal, and spiritual entity. Through this combination, the world was formed: the heavy material bodies sank down, while the ethereal atoms (or fiery ether) rose and spread into the upper celestial regions. Predating modern science by over 2,000 years, he taught that the stars were made of the same material as our Earth and that the sun was a glowing mass. He also taught that the moon was a dark, uninhabitable body that received its light from the sun. Beyond the aforementioned science, he confessed that he was thoroughly convinced that the *real existence of things* perceived by our senses could not be demonstrably proven. He died in exile at Lampsacus at the age of seventy-two.

**Anima Mundi** (*Lat*.) The “Soul of the World,” which is the same as the *Alaya* of the Northern Buddhists. It is the divine Essence that pervades, permeates, animates, and informs all things, from the smallest atom of matter to man and god. In a sense, it is “the seven-skinned Mother” mentioned in the stanzas of the *Secret Doctrine*—the essence of seven planes of sentience, consciousness, and differentiation, both moral and physical. In its highest aspect, it is *Nirvana*; in its lowest, it is the Astral Light. It

When it is said that every human soul was born by detaching itself from the *Anima Mundi*, it means, esoterically, that our higher Egos are of an essence identical with *It*, and *Mahat* is a radiation of the ever-unknown Universal ABSOLUTE.

**Anoia** (Gr.) is a "lack of understanding" or "folly"; it is the name applied by Plato and others to the lower *Manas* when it is too closely allied with *Kama*, which is characterized by irrationality (*agnoia*). The Greek *agnoia* is clearly a derivative of the Sanskrit *ajnana* (phonetically *agnyana*), meaning ignorance, irrationality, and an absence of knowledge.

**Anthropomorphism.** From the Greek *Anthropos*, meaning "man." The act of endowing God or the gods with a human form and human attributes or qualities.

**Anugita** (Sans.) One of the Upanishads; a very occult treatise. (See the Clarendon Press series "The Sacred Books of the East.")

**Apollo Belvedere.** Of all the ancient statues of Apollo—the son of Jupiter and Latona, called Phoebus, Helios the radiant, and the Sun—the best and most perfect is the one of this name, which is in the Belvedere Gallery in the Vatican, at Rome. It is called the *Pythian Apollo*, as the god is represented at the moment of his victory over the serpent Python. The statue was found in the ruins of Antium in 1503.

**Apollonius of Tyana.** A remarkable philosopher born in Cappadocia around the beginning of the first century. He was an ardent Pythagorean who studied the Phoenician sciences under Euthydemus, and Pythagorean philosophy and other subjects under Euxenus of Heraclea. According to the tenets of the Pythagorean school, he remained a vegetarian throughout his long life, eating only fruit and herbs, drinking no wine, wearing garments made only of plant fibers, walking barefoot, and letting his hair...

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

...grow to the full length, as all the Initiates have done before and after him. He was initiated by the priests of the temple of Asclepius (Asclepios) at Aegae, and learned many of the "miracles" for healing the sick performed by the God of medicine. Having prepared himself for a higher initiation through five years of silence and by travel—visiting Antioch, Ephesus, Pamphylia, and other parts—he traveled *via* Babylon to India alone, as all his disciples had abandoned him because they feared to go to the "land of enchantments." However, a casual disciple named Damis, whom he met on his way, accompanied him on his travels. At Babylon, he was initiated by the Chaldeans and Magi, according to Damis, whose narrative was copied by someone named Philostratus one hundred years later. After his return from India, he showed himself to be a true Initiate in that the pestilence, earthquakes, deaths of kings, and other events which he prophesied actually occurred.

At Lesbos, the priests of Orpheus became jealous of him and refused to initiate him into their specific mysteries, though they did so several years later. He preached the purest and noblest ethics to the people of Athens and other states, and the phenomena he produced were as wonderful as they were numerous and well-documented. "How is it," Justin Martyr asks in dismay, "how is it that the talismans (*telesmata*) of Apollonius have power? For they prevent, as we see, the fury of the waves, the violence of the winds, and the attacks of wild beasts; and while *our Lord's miracles are preserved by tradition alone, those of Apollonius are most numerous and actually manifested in present facts?*" (Quest. XXIV.) But an answer to this is easily found in the fact that, after crossing the Hindu Kush, Apollonius had been directed by a king to the *abode of the Sages*, where they may still live to this day, and who taught him their unsurpassed knowledge. His dialogues with the Corinthian Menippus truly give us the esoteric catechism and disclose (when understood) many an important mystery of nature. Apollonius was the friend, correspondent, and guest of kings and queens, and no wonderful

# GLOSSARY.

...or "magic" powers are better attested than his. Towards the close of his long and wonderful life, he opened an esoteric school at Ephesus and died at the ripe old age of one hundred years.

**Archangel.** Highest, supreme angel. From the two Greek words, *arch*, "first," and *angelos*, "messenger."

**Arhat** (*Sanskrit*), also pronounced and written Arahat, Arhan, Rahat, etc., "the worthy one"; a perfected Arya, one exempt from reincarnation; "deserving Divine honors." This was the name first given to the Jain, and subsequently to the Buddhist holy men initiated into the esoteric mysteries. The Arhat is one who has entered the last and highest path, and is thus emancipated from rebirth.

**Arians.** The followers of Arius, a presbyter of the Church in Alexandria in the fourth century. One who holds that Christ is a created and human being, inferior to God the Father, though a grand and noble man, a true adept, versed in all the divine mysteries.

**Aristobulus.** An Alexandrian writer and an obscure philosopher. A Jew who tried to prove that Aristotle explained the esoteric thoughts of Moses.

**Aryan** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, "the holy"; those who had mastered the Aryasatyani and entered the Aryamarga path to Nirvana or Moksha, the great "fourfold" path. They were originally known as Rishis. But now the name has become the epithet of a race, and our Orientalists, depriving the Hindu Brahmans of their birthright, have made Aryans of all Europeans. Since, in esotericism, the four paths or stages can only be entered through great spiritual development and "growth in holiness," they are called the Aryamarga. The degrees of Arhatship, called respectively Srotâpatti, Sakridâgâmin, Anâgâmin, and Arhat, or the four classes of Aryas, correspond to the four paths and truths.

**Aspect.** The form (*rupa*) under which any principle in septenary man or nature manifests is called an *aspect* of that principle in Theosophy.

<meta>This page continues the glossary from page 317, following the entry for "Aspect."</meta>

# GLOSSARY.

**Astral Body.** The ethereal counterpart or double of any physical body.—*Doppelgänger*.

**Astrology.** The science that defines the action of celestial bodies upon earthly affairs, and claims to foretell future events from the positions of the stars. Its antiquity is such that it is placed among the very earliest records of human learning. For long ages it remained a secret science in the East, and its ultimate expression remains so to this day; its esoteric application has only been brought to any degree of perfection in the West during the time since Varaha Mihira wrote his book on astrology, some 1,400 years ago. Claudius Ptolemy, the famous geographer and mathematician who founded the system of astronomy known by his name, wrote his *Tetrabiblos*, which is still the basis of modern astrology, in 135 A.D. The science of horoscopy is studied now chiefly under four heads, namely: (1). *Mundane*, in its application to meteorology, seismology, and agriculture. (2). *State or Civic*, in regard to the future of nations, kings, and rulers. (3). *Horary*, in reference to the solving of doubts arising in the mind upon any subject. (4.) *Genethliacal*, in connection with the future of individuals from birth until death. The Egyptians and the Chaldeans were among the most ancient devotees of astrology, though their modes of reading the stars and modern methods differ considerably. The former claimed that Belus—the Bel or Elu of the Chaldeans, a descendant of the *Divine Dynasty*, or the dynasty of the King-gods—had belonged to the land of Chemi, and had left it to found a colony from Egypt on the banks of the Euphrates, where a temple, served by priests in the service of the "lords of the stars," was built. As to the origin of the science, it is known on the one hand that Thebes claimed the honor of inventing astrology; whereas, on the other hand, all are agreed that it was the Chaldeans who taught that science to other nations. Now Thebes predated considerably not only "Ur of the Chaldees," but also

All this, therefore, tends to support the Egyptian claim. If later on the name of Astrologer fell into disrepute in Rome and elsewhere, it was due to the frauds of those who wanted to make money from that which was part and parcel of the Sacred Science of the Mysteries; and who, ignorant of the latter, developed a system based entirely on mathematics instead of transcendental metaphysics with the physical celestial bodies as its *upadhi* or material basis. Yet, despite all persecutions, the number of adherents to Astrology among the most intellectual and scientific minds was always very great. If Cardan and Kepler were among its ardent supporters, then later devotees have nothing to be ashamed of, even in its now imperfect and distorted form. As said in *Isis Unveiled* (I., 259), "Astrology is to exact astronomy, what psychology is to exact physiology. In astrology and psychology one has to step beyond the visible world of matter and enter into the domain of transcendent spirit."

**Athenagoras.** A Platonic Philosopher of Athens, who wrote an apology for the Christians in 177 A.D., addressed to Marcus Aurelius, to prove that the accusations brought against them—namely, that they were incestuous and ate murdered children—were untrue.

**Atman, or Atma (*Sans.*)** The Universal Spirit, the divine monad, "the seventh Principle," so called, in the exoteric "septenary" classification of man. The Supreme Soul.

**Aura (*Gr. and Lat.*)** A subtle invisible essence or fluid that emanates from human, animal, and other bodies. It is a psychic emanation partaking of both the mind and the body, as there is both an electro-vital and at the same time an electro-mental aura; called in Theosophy the Akasic or magnetic aura. In Roman Catholic Martyrology, a Saint.

## B.

**Beness.** A term coined by Theosophists to render more accurately the essential meaning of the untranslatable word <term>*Sat*</term>. The latter word does not mean “Being,” for the term “Being” presupposes a sentient consciousness of existence. But as the term <term>*Sat*</term> is applied solely to the absolute principle—that universal, unknown, and ever-unknowable principle which philosophical Pantheism postulates, calling it the basic root of the Kosmos and the Kosmos itself—it could not be translated by the simple term “Being.” <term>*Sat*</term>, indeed, is not even “the incomprehensible Entity,” as translated by some Orientalists; for it is no more an “Entity” than a non-entity, but both. It is, as stated, absolute BENESS, not “Being”; the one, secondless, undivided and indivisible ALL—the root of nature, both visible and invisible, objective and subjective, comprehensible and—never to be fully comprehended.

**Bhagavat-Gita** (*Sans.*) Literally, “the Lord’s Song,” a portion of the <term>*Mahabharata*</term>, the great epic poem of India. It contains a dialogue in which Krishna—the “Charioteer”—and Arjuna, his <term>*chela*</term>, have a discussion upon the highest spiritual philosophy. The work is pre-eminently occult or esoteric.
