**Boëhme (Jacob).** A mystic and great philosopher, one of the most prominent Theosophists of the medieval ages. He was born about 1575 at Old Diedenberg, some two miles from Görlitz (Silesia), and died in 1624, being nearly fifty years old. When a boy, he was a common shepherd, and, after learning to read and write in a village school, became an apprentice to a poor shoemaker at Görlitz. He was a natural clairvoyant of the most wonderful power. With no education or acquaintance with science, he wrote works which are now proved to be full of scientific truths; but these, as he himself says of what he wrote, he "saw as in a Great Deep in the Eternal." He had "a thorough view of the universe, as in chaos," which yet opened itself in him, from time to time, "as in a young planet," he says. He was a born mystic through and through, and evidently of a constitution which is most rare; one of those fine natures whose material envelope in no way impedes the direct, even if only occasional, communication between the intellectual and spiritual <term>Ego</term>. It is this <term>*Ego*</term> which Jacob Boëhme, like so many other untrained mystics, mistook for God. "Man must acknowledge," he writes, "that his knowledge is not his own, but from God, who manifests the Ideas of Wisdom to the Soul of Man in whatever measure he pleases." Had this great Theosophist been born 300 years later, he might have expressed it differently. He would have known that the "God" who spoke through his poor uncultured and untrained brain was his own Divine <term>Ego</term>, the omniscient Deity within himself, and that what that Deity gave out was not "in whatever measure he pleased," but in the measure of the capacities of the mortal and temporary dwelling it animated.

**Book of the Keys.** An ancient <term>Kabalistic</term> work. The original is no longer in existence, though there may be fake and distorted copies and forgeries of it.

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

Brahma, and the male Creator of the Indian Pantheon, Brahmâ. The former Brahma or Brahman is the impersonal, Supreme, and unrecognizable Soul of the Universe, from the essence of which all emanates, and into which all returns; which is incorporeal, immaterial, unborn, eternal, without beginning and without end. It is all-pervading, animating the highest god as well as the smallest mineral atom. Brahmâ, on the other hand, the male and the alleged Creator, exists in his manifestation periodically only, and passes into <term>pralaya</term>, that is, disappears and is annihilated just as periodically. (See below.)

**Brahmâ's Day.** A period of 2,160,000,000 years, during which Brahmâ, having emerged out of his Golden Egg (Hiranya Garbha), creates and fashions the material world (as he is simply the fertilizing and creative force in Nature). After this period, the worlds being destroyed in turn by fire and water, he vanishes with objective nature; and then comes

**Brahmâ's Night.** A period of equal duration, in which Brahmâ is said to sleep. Upon awakening he recommences the process, and this goes on for an AGE of Brahmâ composed of alternate “Days” and “Nights,” and lasting for 100 years of 2,160,000,000 each. It requires fifteen figures to express the duration of such an age, after the expiration of which the <term>Mahapralaya</term> or Great Dissolution sets in, and lasts in its turn for the same space of fifteen figures.

**Brahm-Vidya** (*Sanskrit*) The knowledge or Esoteric Science concerning the true nature of the two Brahmas.

**Buddha** (*Sanskrit*) “The enlightened.” Generally known as the title of Gautama Buddha, the Prince of Kapilavastu, the founder of modern Buddhism. The highest degree of knowledge and holiness. To become a Buddha one has to break through the bondage of sense and personality; to acquire a complete perception of the *real Self*, and learn not to separate it from all the other Selves; to learn by experience the utter unreality of all phenomena, foremost of all the visible Cosmos; to

<meta>This page continues the glossary entry for Buddha and defines Buddhi and Buddhism, distinguishing between the Southern and Northern schools of the religion.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

**Buddhi** (*Sans.*) Universal Soul or Mind. Mahabuddhi is a name of Mahat (see that entry); also the Spiritual Soul in man (the sixth principle exoterically), the vehicle of Atma, the seventh, according to the exoteric enumeration.

**Buddhism** is the religious philosophy taught by Gautama Buddha. It is now split into two distinct churches: the Southern and the Northern. The former is said to be the purer, as it has more religiously preserved the original teachings of the Lord Buddha. Northern Buddhism is confined to Tibet, China, and Nepal. But this distinction is incorrect. If the Southern Church is closer to—and has not, in fact, departed from—the public or exoteric teachings of Sakyamuni (except perhaps in minor dogmas due to the many councils held after the death of the MASTER), then the Northern Church is the result of Siddhartha Buddha’s esoteric teachings, which he restricted to his chosen Bhikshus and Arhats. Buddhism, in fact, cannot be justly judged in our age by either of its exoteric popular forms. Real Buddhism can be appreciated only by blending the philosophy of the Southern Church and the metaphysics of the Northern Schools. If one seems too iconoclastic and stern, and the other too metaphysical and transcendental—the latter being overcharged with the weeds of Indian exotericism, many of the gods of its Pantheon having been transplanted under new names into Tibetan soil—it is due to the popular expression of Buddhism in both churches. Correspondingly, they stand in their relation to each other as Protestantism does to Roman Catholicism. Both err by an excess of zeal and erroneous interpretations, though neither the Southern nor the Northern Buddhist clergy have ever departed from the Truth consciously. Still less have they acted under the dictates of a priest-led rule, ambition, or an eye for personal gain and power, as the later churches have.

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

## **Buddhi-Taijasi** (Sans.) A very mystical term, capable of several interpretations. In Occultism, however, and in relation to the human “Principles” (exoterically), it is a term to express the state of our dual Manas, when, reunited during a man’s life, it bathes in the radiance of Buddhi, the Spiritual Soul. For “*Taijasi*” means *the radiant*, and Manas, becoming radiant in consequence of its union with Buddhi, and being, so to speak, merged into it, is identified with the latter; the trinity has become one; and, as the element of Buddhi is the highest, it becomes *Buddhi-Taijasi*. In short, it is the human soul illuminated by the radiance of the divine soul, the human reason lit by the light of the Spirit or *Divine SELF-CONSCIOUSNESS*.

# C.

## **Caste.** Originally the system of the four hereditary classes into which the Indian population was divided: Brahman, Kshatriya, Vaisya, and Shoodra—(*a*) descendants of Brahma; (*b*) warrior; (*c*) mercantile, and (*d*) the lowest or agricultural Shoodra class. From these four, hundreds of divisions and minor castes have emerged.
## **Causal Body.** This “body,” which is in reality no body at all, either objective or subjective, but Buddhi the Spiritual Soul, is so named because it is the direct cause of the *Sushupti* state leading to the *Turya* state, the highest state of *Samadhi*. It is called *Karanopadhi*, “the basis of the cause,” by the “*Taraka Raj*” Yogis, and in the *Vedanta* System corresponds to both the *Vignananamaya* and *Anandamaya Kosha* (the latter coming next to Atma, and therefore being the vehicle of the Universal Spirit). Buddhi alone could not be called a “Causal body,” but becomes one in conjunction with Manas, the incarnating Entity or Ego.
## **Chela** (Sans.) A disciple. The pupil of a Guru or Sage, the follower of some Adept, or a school of philosophy.

<meta>This page continues the glossary entries under the letter “C,” providing an in-depth definition of the term Chrestos and its historical and mystical relationship to the term Christos.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

**Chrestos** (*Gr.*) The early Gnostic term for Christ. This technical term was used in the fifth century B.C. by Aeschylus, Herodotus, and others. The *Manteumata pythocresta*, or the “Oracles delivered by a Pythian God” through a priestess (pythoness), are mentioned by the former (Cho. 901), and *Pythochrestos* is derived from *chrao*. *Chresterion* is not only “the test of an oracle,” but an offering to, or for, the oracle. *Chrestes* is one who explains oracles, a “prophet and soothsayer,” and *Chresterios* is one who serves an oracle or a God. The earliest Christian writer, Justin Martyr, in his first Apology, calls his fellow believers *Chrestians*. “It is only through ignorance that men call themselves Christians, instead of Chrestians,” says Lactantius (*book IV*, *chapter VII*). The terms Christ and Christians, spelled originally *Chrest* and *Chrestians*, were borrowed from the Temple vocabulary of the Pagans. In that vocabulary, *Chrestos* meant “a disciple on probation,” a candidate for hierophantship; who, when he had attained it through initiation, long trials, and suffering, and had been anointed (*i.e.*, “rubbed with oil,” as initiates and even idols of the Gods were, as the final touch of ritual observance), was changed into *Christos*—the “purified” in esoteric or mystery language. In mystic symbology, indeed, *Christes* or *Christos* meant that the “way,” the *Path*, was already trodden and the goal reached; when the fruits of the arduous labor, uniting the personality of evanescent clay with the indestructible INDIVIDUALITY, transformed it thereby into the immortal EGO. “At the end of the way stands the Christos,” the Purifier; and once the union was accomplished, the *Chrestos*, the “man of sorrow,” became *Christos* himself. Paul, the Initiate, knew this and meant exactly this when he is made to say in a poor translation, “I labor in birth again until Christ be formed in you” (Gal. iv., 19), the true rendering of which is, “. . . until you form the <term>Christos</term> within yourselves.” But the profane, who knew only that *Chrestos* was in some way connected with priest and prophet, and knew nothing about the hidden meaning of <term>Christos</term>, insisted, as did Lactantius and Justin

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>
**Martyr,** on being called *Chrestians* instead of Christ. Every good individual, therefore, may find Christ in his “inner man,” as Paul expresses it (Ephesians 3:16–17), whether he is a Jew, Muslim, Hindu, or Christian.
**Christ** (see CHRESTOS).
**Christian Scientist.** A newly coined term for denoting the practitioners of a healing art by the will. The name is a misnomer, since a Buddhist or Jew, Hindu or Materialist can practice this new form of *Western Yoga* with equal success if he can only guide and control his will with sufficient firmness. “Mental Scientists” is another rival school. These work by a universal denial of every disease and evil imaginable, and claim, syllogistically, that since the Universal Spirit cannot be subject to the ailments of the flesh, and since every atom is Spirit and in Spirit, and since, finally, they—the healers and the healed—are all absorbed in this Spirit or Deity, there is not, nor can there be, such a thing as disease. This in no way prevents both Christian and Mental Scientists from succumbing to disease and nursing chronic illnesses for years in their own bodies, just like other ordinary mortals.
**Clairaudience.** The faculty—whether innate or acquired by occult training—to hear things at any distance.
**Clairvoyance.** A faculty of seeing with the inner eye or spiritual sight. As now used, it is a loose and flippant term, embracing both a happy guess due to natural shrewdness or intuition, and also that faculty which was so remarkably exercised by Jacob Boehme and Swedenborg. Yet even these two great seers, since they could never rise above the general spirit of the Jewish Bible and sectarian teachings, have sadly confused what they saw and fallen far short of true clairvoyance.
**Clemens Alexandrinus.** A Church Father and prolific writer who had been a Neo-Platonist and a disciple of Ammonius Saccas. He was one of the few Christian philosophers between the second and third centuries of our era in Alexandria.

**College of Rabbis.** A college at Babylon; it was most famous during the early centuries of Christianity, but its prestige was greatly overshadowed by the appearance of Hellenistic teachers in Alexandria, such as Philo-Judæus, Josephus, Aristobulus, and others. The former took revenge on their successful rivals by speaking of the Alexandrians as theurgists and unclean prophets. However, the Alexandrian believers in thaumaturgy were not regarded as sinners and impostors during the time when orthodox Jews headed such schools of <term>hazim</term> <note>seers</note>. There were colleges for teaching prophecy and the occult sciences. Samuel was the head of such a college at Ramah, and Elisha at Jericho. Hillel had a regular academy for prophets and seers; it was Hillel, a student of the Babylonian College, who was the founder of the sect of the Pharisees and the lineage of the great orthodox Rabbis.

**Cycle** (Greek) *KUKLOS*. The ancients divided time into endless cycles—wheels within wheels—all such periods being of various durations, and each marking the beginning or end of some event, whether cosmic, worldly, physical, or metaphysical. There were cycles of only a few years and cycles of immense duration. The great Orphic cycle, referring to the ethnological change of races, lasts 120,000 years, and that of Cassandrus lasts 136,000 years; the latter brought about a complete change in planetary influences and their correlations between men and gods—a fact entirely ignored by modern astrologers.

## D.

**Deist.** One who admits the possibility of the existence of a God or gods but claims to know nothing of them and denies divine revelation. An ancient version of an agnostic.

**Deva** (Sanskrit). A god, a “resplendent” Deity; *Deva-Deus* comes from the root *div*, “to shine.” A Deva is a celestial being—whether good, bad or

**Devachan** (*Sanskrit*) The “Dwelling of the Gods.” A state intermediate between two earth-lives, into which the Ego (Atma-Buddhi-Manas, or the Trinity made one) enters after its separation from Kama Rupa and the disintegration of the lower principles, following the death of the body on Earth.

**Dhammapada** (*Sanskrit*) A work containing various aphorisms from the Buddhist Scriptures.

**Dhyana** (*Sanskrit*) One of the six Paramitas of perfection. A state of abstraction which carries the practicing ascetic far above the region of sensory perception and out of the world of matter. Literally, “contemplation.” The six stages of Dhyan differ only in the degrees of abstraction of the personal Ego from sensory life.

**Dhyan Chohans** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, “The Lords of Light.” The highest gods, corresponding to the Roman Catholic Archangels. The divine Intelligences charged with the supervision of the Kosmos.

**Double.** The same as the Astral body or “Doppelganger.”

### E.

**Ecstasis** (*Greek*) A psycho-spiritual state; a physical trance which induces clairvoyance and a beatific state which brings on visions.

**Ego** (*Latin*) “I”; the consciousness in man of the “I am I,” or the feeling of "I-am-ship." Esoteric philosophy teaches the existence of two Egos in man: the mortal or *personal*, and the higher, the divine or impersonal, calling the former “personality” and the latter “individuality.”

**Egoity** (from the word “Ego”). Egoity means “individuality”—

**Eidolon** (Greek) The same as what we call the human phantom, the Astral form.

**Elementals, or Spirits of the Elements.** The creatures evolved in the Four Kingdoms, or Elements—Earth, Air, Fire, and Water. They are called by the Kabalists: Gnomes (of the Earth), Sylphs (of the Air), Salamanders (of the Fire), and Undines (of the Water), with the exception of a few of the higher kinds and their rulers. They are forces of nature rather than ethereal men and women. These forces, as the subservient agents of the occultist, may produce various effects; but if employed by elementaries (*Kamarupas*)—in which case they enslave the mediums—they will deceive. All the lower invisible beings generated on the fifth, sixth, and seventh Planes of our terrestrial atmosphere are called Elementals—Peris, Devs, Djins, Sylvans, Satyrs, Fauns, Elves, Dwarfs, Trolls, Norns, Kobolds, Brownies, Nixies, Goblins, Pinkies, Banshees, Moss People, White Ladies, Spooks, Fairies, etc., etc.

**Eleusinia** (Greek) The Eleusinian Mysteries were the most famous and the most ancient of all the Greek mysteries (except for the Samothracian), and were performed near the village of Eleusis, not far from Athens. Epiphanius traces them back to the days of Iacchos (1800 B.C.). They were held in honor of Demeter (the great Ceres) and the Egyptian Isis; the last act of the performance referred to a sacrificial victim of atonement and a resurrection, at which point the Initiate was admitted to the highest degree of Epopt. The festival of the Mysteries began in the month of Boëdromion (September), the time of the grape harvest, and lasted from the 15th to the 22nd—seven days. The Hebrew Feast of Tabernacles—the festival of harvest—in the month of Ethanim (the seventh month) also began on the 15th and ended on the 22nd of that month. The name of the month (Ethanim) is derived, according to some, from Adonim, Adonia, Attenim, and Ethanim, and was in honor

**Emanation** (the doctrine of) is, in its metaphysical meaning, opposed to evolution, yet one with it. Science teaches that, physiologically, evolution is a method of generation in which the germ that develops the fetus already pre-exists in the parent, the development and final form and characteristics of that germ being accomplished by nature; and that (as in its cosmology) the process takes place blindly, through the correlation of the elements and their various compounds. Occultism teaches that this is only the apparent method, the real process being Emanation, guided by intelligent forces under an immutable LAW. Therefore, while the Occultists and Theosophists believe thoroughly in the doctrine of Evolution as given out by Kapila and Manu, they are *Emanationists* rather than *Evolutionists*. The doctrine of Emanation was at one time universal. It was taught by the Alexandrian, as well as by the Indian philosophers, by the Egyptian, the Chaldean, and Hellenic Hierophants, and also by the Hebrews (in their Kabala, and even in Genesis). For it is only owing to deliberate mistranslation that the Hebrew word *asdt* was translated as “angels” from the Septuagint, while it actually means Emanations, Aeons, just as it does with the Gnostics. Indeed, in Deuteronomy (33:2) the word *asdt* or *ashdi* is translated as “fiery law,” while the correct rendering of the passage should be, “from his right hand went (not a fiery law, but) a fire according to law,” namely, that the fire of one flame is imparted to and caught up by another—much like in a trail of inflammable substance. This is precisely Emanation, as shown in *Isis Unveiled*.

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

# F.

**Ferho** (*Gnostic*). The highest and greatest creative power among the Nazarene Gnostics (*Codex Nazaraeus*).

**Fire-Philosophers**. The name given to the Hermetists and Alchemists of the Middle Ages, and also to the Rosicrucians. The latter, the successors of the Theurgists, regarded fire as the symbol of Deity. It was the source, not only of material atoms, but the container of the spiritual and psychic forces energizing them. Broadly analyzed, fire is a triple principle; esoterically, it is a septenary, as are all the rest of the elements. Just as man is composed of spirit, soul, and body, *plus* a fourfold aspect, so is fire. As in the works of Robert Flood (de Fluctibus), one of the famous Rosicrucians, fire contains—firstly, a visible flame (body); secondly, an invisible, astral fire (soul); and thirdly, spirit. The four aspects are (*a*) heat (life), (*b*) light (mind), (*c*) electricity (Kamic or molecular powers), and (*d*) the synthetic essences, *beyond* spirit, or the radical cause of its existence and manifestation. For the Hermetist or Rosicrucian, when a flame is extinguished on the objective plane, it has only passed from the visible world into the unseen; from the knowable into the unknowable.

# G.

**Gautama** (*Sanskrit*). A name in India. It is that of the Prince of Kapilavastu, son of Suddhodana, the Shakya King of a small territory on the borders of Nepal, born in the seventh century B.C., now called

the “Saviour of the world.” Gautama or Gotama was the priestly name of the Shakya family. Born a simple mortal, he rose to Buddhaship through his own personal and unaided merit; a man—truly greater than any God!

**Gebirol.** Salomon Ben Jehudah, called in literature *Avicebron*. An Israelite by birth, a philosopher, poet, and Kabbalist; a voluminous writer and a mystic. He was born in the eleventh century at Malaga (1021), educated at Saragossa, and died at Valencia in 1070, murdered by a Muslim. His fellow-religionists called him Salomon, the Sephardi, or the Spaniard, and the Arabs called him Abu Ayyub Suleiman-ben ya’hya Ibn Dgebirol, while the Scholastics named him *Avicebron* (see Myers’ *Quabbalah*). Ibn Gebirol was certainly one of the greatest philosophers and scholars of his age. He wrote much in Arabic, and most of his manuscripts have been preserved. His greatest work appears to be *The Megôr Hayyim*, that is, *The Fountain of Life*, “one of the earliest exposures of the secrets of the Speculative Kabbalah,” as his biographer informs us.

**Gnosis** (*Greek*). Literally “knowledge.” The technical term used by the schools of religious philosophy, both before and during the first centuries of so-called Christianity, to denote the object of their inquiry. This spiritual and sacred knowledge, the *Gupta Vidya* of the Hindus, could only be obtained by initiation into spiritual mysteries, of which the ceremonial “mysteries” were a type.

**Gnostics** (*Greek*). The philosophers who formulated and taught the “Gnosis” or knowledge. They flourished in the first three centuries of the Christian Era. The following were eminent: Valentinus, Basilides, Marcion, Simon Magus, etc.

**Golden Age.** The ancients divided the life cycle into the Golden, Silver, Bronze, and Iron Ages. The Golden was an age of primeval purity, simplicity, and general happiness.

**Great Age.** There were several “Great Ages” mentioned by the

ancients. In India, it encompassed the whole Maha-Manvantara, the “Age of Brahmâ,” each “Day” of which represents the life cycle of a chain—that is, it covers a period of Seven Rounds (see “Esoteric Buddhism” by A. P. Sinnett). Thus, while a “Day” and a “Night” represent, as Manvantara and Pralaya, 8,640,000,000 years, an “age” lasts through a period of 311,040,000,000,000 years; after which the *Pralaya*, or dissolution of the universe, becomes universal. For the Egyptians and Greeks, the “Great Age” referred only to the tropical or sidereal year, the duration of which is 25,868 solar years. Regarding the complete age—that of the Gods—they said nothing, as it was a matter to be discussed and revealed only at the Mysteries and during the initiation ceremonies. The “Great Age” of the Chaldeans was numerically the same as that of the Hindus.

**Guhya Vidya** (*Sanskrit*). The secret knowledge of mystic mantras.
**Gupta Vidya** (*Sanskrit*). The same as *Guhya Vidya*. Esoteric or secret science and knowledge.
**Gyges.** “The ring of Gyges” has become a familiar metaphor in European literature. Gyges was a Lydian who, after murdering King Candaules, married his widow. Plato tells us that Gyges, once descending into a chasm in the earth, discovered a bronze horse; inside its open side was the skeleton of a giant man who wore a bronze ring on his finger. This ring, when placed on his own finger, made him invisible.

purely karmic. Neither Hades nor Amenti was the Hell still preached by some retrograde priests and clergymen; and whether represented by the Elysian Fields or by Tartarus, they could only be reached by crossing the river to the “other shore.” As well expressed in the “*Egyptian Belief*,” the story of Charon, the ferryman (of the Styx), is found not only in Homer but in the poetry of many lands. The *River* must be crossed before reaching the *Isles of the Blest*. The *Ritual of Egypt* described a Charon and his boat long ages before Homer. He is Khu-en-na, “the hawk-headed steersman.” (See *Hell*.)

**Hallucinations.** A state produced sometimes by physiological disorders, sometimes by mediumship, and at others by drunkenness. But the cause that produces the visions must be sought deeper than physiology. All such states, particularly when produced through mediumship, are preceded by a relaxation of the nervous system, invariably generating an abnormal magnetic condition which attracts waves of astral light to the sufferer. It is these latter that provide the various hallucinations, which, however, are not always—as physicians would explain them—mere empty and unreal dreams. No one can see that which does not exist—*that is*, which is not impressed—in or on the astral waves. But a seer may perceive objects and scenes (whether past, present, or future) which have no relation whatever to himself; and perceive, moreover, several things entirely disconnected from each other at one and the same time, so as to produce the most grotesque and absurd combinations. But drunkard and seer, medium and adept see their respective visions in the astral light; only while the drunkard, the madman, and the untrained medium, or one in a brain fever, see because they cannot help it and evoke jumbled visions unconsciously to themselves without being able to control them, the adept and the trained Seer have the choice and the control of such visions. They know where to fix their gaze, how to steady the scenes they wish to observe, and how to see beyond the upper outward layers of the astral light. With the former, such glimpses into

the *waves* are hallucinations; with the latter, they become the faithful reproduction of what actually has been, is, or will be taking place. The random glimpses caught by the medium, and his flickering visions in the deceptive light, are transformed under the guiding will of the adept and seer into steady pictures—the truthful representation of what he wills to come within the focus of his perception.

**Hell.** A term which the Anglo-Saxon race has evidently derived from the name of the Scandinavian goddess, *Hela*, just as the word *ad* in Russian and other Slavic tongues, expressing the same conception, is derived from the Greek *Hades*. The only difference between the Scandinavian cold Hell and the hot Hell of the Christians is found in their respective temperatures. But even the idea of these overheated regions did not originate with the Europeans, as many people have entertained the conception of an underworld climate—as we well might, if we localize our Hell in the center of the earth. All exoteric religions—the creeds of the Brahmins, Buddhists, Zoroastrians, Muslims, Jews, and the rest—made their Hells hot and dark, though many were more attractive than frightful. The idea of a hot Hell is an afterthought, the distortion of an astronomical allegory. With the Egyptians, Hell became a place of punishment by fire no earlier than the 17th or 18th Dynasty, when Typhon was transformed from a god into a devil. But at whatever time they implanted this dread superstition in the minds of the poor, ignorant masses, the scheme of a burning Hell and souls tormented within it is purely Egyptian. Ra (the Sun) became the Lord of the Furnace in *Karr*, the Hell of the Pharaohs, and the sinner was threatened with misery "in the heat of infernal fires." "A lion was there," says Dr. Birch, "and was called the roaring monster." Another describes the place as "the bottomless pit and lake of fire, into which the victims are thrown" (compare *Revelation*). The Hebrew word *gaï-hinnom* (Gehenna) never really had the significance given to it in Christian orthodoxy.

**Hermas**, an ancient Greek writer, of whose works only a few fragments currently remain.

**Hierogrammatists** (*Gr.*) The title given to those Egyptian priests who were entrusted with writing and reading the sacred and secret records. Literally, the “scribes of the secret records.” They were the instructors of the neophytes preparing for initiation.

**Hierophant.** From the Greek *Hierophantes*, literally “he who explains sacred things”; a title belonging to the highest adepts in the temples of antiquity, who were the teachers and explainers of the Mysteries, and the initiators into the final great Mysteries. The Hierophant represented the Demiurge and explained to the candidates for initiation the various phenomena of creation that were produced for their instruction. “He was the only interpreter of the exoteric secrets and doctrines. It was forbidden even to pronounce his name in the presence of an uninitiated person. He sat in the East and wore, as a symbol of authority, a golden globe suspended from the neck. He was also called Mystagogus.” (Kennett R. H. Mackenzie, IX., F.T.S., in *The Royal Masonic Cyclopædia*.)

**Hillel.** A great Babylonian Rabbi of the century before the Christian Era. He was the founder of the sect of the Pharisees, a learned and saintly man.

**Hinayana** (*Sans.*) The “Smaller Vehicle”; a Scripture and a School of the Buddhists, contrasted with the Mahayana, or “The Greater Vehicle.” Both schools are mystical. (See Mahayana.) Also in exoteric superstition, it refers to the lowest form of rebirth.

**Homogeneity.** From the Greek words *homos*, “the same”; and *genos*, “kind.” That which is of the same nature throughout, undifferentiated and non-compound, just as gold is thought to be.

**Hypnotism** (*Gr.*) A name given by Dr. Braid to the process by which one person of strong willpower puts another of weaker mind into a type of trance; once in such a state, the latter will do anything suggested

## I.

**Iamblicus.** A great Theosophist and an Initiate of the third century. He wrote extensively about the various kinds of demons who appear through evocation, but spoke severely against such phenomena. His self-discipline, purity of life, and earnestness were great. He is credited with having been levitated ten cubits high from the ground, just as some modern Yogis and mediums are.

**Illusion.** In Occultism, everything finite (such as the Universe and all within it) is called Illusion or *Maya*.

**Individuality.** One of the names given in Theosophy and Occultism to the human Higher Ego. We distinguish between the immortal and divine Ego and the mortal human Ego, which perishes. The latter, or "Personality" (personal Ego), survives the dead body for only a time in Kama Loka; the Individuality prevails forever.

**Initiate.** From the Latin *Initiatus*. The designation for anyone who was received into and had revealed to him the mysteries and secrets of either Masonry or Occultism. In antiquity, these were individuals who had been initiated into the arcane knowledge taught by the Hierophants of the Mysteries; in our modern day, they are those who have been initiated by the adepts of mystic lore into the mysterious knowledge which, despite the passage of ages, still has a few true devotees on earth.

**Iswara (Sans.)** The "Lord" or the personal god, the divine spirit *within man*. Literally sovereign (independent) existence. A title given to

### K.

**Kabbalah** *(Hebrew)*, or Kabbala. "The hidden wisdom of the Hebrew Rabbis of the Middle Ages, derived from the older secret doctrines concerning divine things and cosmogony, which were combined into a theology after the time of the captivity of the Jews in Babylon." All the works that fall under this esoteric category are termed Kabbalistic.

**Kamaloka** *(Sanskrit)*. The semi-material plane, which is subjective and invisible to us, where the disembodied "personalities"—the astral forms called Kama Rupa—remain until they fade out from it through the complete exhaustion of the effects of the mental impulses that created these eidolons of the lower animal passions and desires. (See Kama Rupa.) It is the Hades of the ancient Greeks and the Amenti of the Egyptians—the land of Silent Shadows.

**Kama Rupa** *(Sanskrit)*. Metaphysically and in our esoteric philosophy, it is the subjective form created through the mental and physical desires and thoughts in connection with material things by all sentient beings: a form which survives the death of its body. After that death, three of the seven "principles"—or, let us say, planes of the senses and consciousness on which the human instincts and ideation act in turn—namely, the body, its astral prototype, and physical vitality, being of no further use, remain on earth. The three higher principles, grouped into one, merge into a state of Devachan (*q.v.*), in which state the Higher Ego will remain until the hour for a new reincarnation arrives, and the eidolon of the former personality is left alone in its new abode. Here the pale copy of the person that was vegetates for a period of time, the duration of which is variable according to the element of materiality which is left in it, and which is determined by the past life of the deceased. Bereft as it is of its

**Kapilavastu** (*Sanskrit*) The birthplace of the <term>Lord Buddha</term>, called the “yellow dwelling,” the capital of the monarch who was the father of <term>Gautama Buddha</term>.

**Karma** (*Sanskrit*) Physically, action; Metaphysically, the LAW of RETRIBUTION; the Law of Cause and Effect or <term>Ethical Causation</term>. It is <term>Nemesis</term> only in the sense of bad <term>Karma</term>. It is the eleventh *Nidana* in the concatenation of causes and effects in orthodox <term>Buddhism</term>; yet it is the power that controls all things, the resultant of moral action, the metaphysical <term>Samskâra</term>, or the moral effect of an act committed for the attainment of something which gratifies a personal desire. There is the <term>Karma</term> of merit and the <term>Karma</term> of demerit. <term>Karma</term> neither punishes nor rewards; it is simply the one Universal LAW which guides unerringly and, so to say, blindly, all other laws productive of certain effects along the grooves of their respective causations. When <term>Buddhism</term> teaches that “<term>Karma</term> is that moral Kernel (of any being) which alone survives death and continues in <term>transmigration</term>” or reincarnation, it simply means that there remains nothing after each personality, but the causes pro-

**Kether** (Hebrew) “The Crown,” the highest of the ten <term>Sephiroth</term>; the first of the heavenly Triad. It corresponds to the <term>Macroprosopus</term>, “Vast Countenance,” or <term>Arikh Anpin</term>, which differentiates into <term>Chokmah</term> and <term>Binah</term>.

**Krishna** (Sanskrit) The most celebrated <term>Avatar</term> of <term>Vishnu</term>, the “Savior” of the Hindus and the most popular god. The missionaries, of course, try to show that the Hindus stole the story of the Nativity from the early Christians who came to India.

**Kshetragna**, or *Kshetragneswara* (Sanskrit) Embodied Spirit in Occultism, the conscious <term>Ego</term> in its highest manifestations; the reincarnating Principle, or the “Lord” within us.

<term>Kumara</term> (*Sans.*) A virgin boy or young celibate. The first Kumaras are the seven sons of Brahmâ, born from the limbs of the god in the so-called Ninth Creation. It is stated that the name was given to them because of their formal refusal to “procreate” their species, and thus they “remained Yogis” according to the legend.

->L.<-

<term>Labro, St.</term> A Roman Saint solemnly beatified a few years ago. His great holiness consisted of sitting at one of the gates of Rome night and day for forty years and remaining unwashed through the whole of that time, the result of which was that he was eaten by vermin to his bones.
<term>Lao-Tze</term> (*Chin.*) A great Sage, Saint, and Philosopher, who preceded Confucius.
<term>Law of Retribution</term> (see <term>Karma</term>).
<term>Linga Sharira</term> (*Sans.*) “Astral body,” i.e., the aerial symbol of the body. This term designates the <term>doppelgänger</term>, or the “astral body” of man or animal. It is the <term>eidolon</term> of the Greeks, the vital and <term>prototypal</term> body, the reflection of the man of flesh. It is born before man and dies or fades out with the disappearance of the last atom of the body.
<term>Logos</term> (*Gr.*) The manifested deity in every nation and people; the outward expression or the effect of the Cause which is always concealed. Thus, speech is the logos of thought; hence, in its metaphysical sense, it is aptly translated by the terms “Verbum,” and the “Word.”
<term>Long Face</term>. A Kabbalistic term, <term>*Areekh Anpeen*</term> in Hebrew; or “Long Face”; in Greek, <term>*Macroprosopos*</term>, as contrasted with “Short Face,” or <term>*Zeir Anpeen*</term>, the <term>*Microprosopos*</term>. One relates to Deity, the other to man, the “little image of the great form.”

<term>Longinus, Dionysius Cassius</term>. A famous critic and philosopher, born at the very beginning of the third century (about 213). He was a great traveler and attended the lectures of Ammonius Saccas in Alexandria, the founder of Neoplatonism, but he was more of a critic than a follower. Porphyry (the Jew Malek or Malchus) was his pupil before he became the disciple of Plotinus. It is said of him that he was a living library and a walking museum. Toward the end of his life, he became the instructor in Greek literature for Zenobia, Queen of Palmyra. She repaid his services by accusing him before the Emperor Aurelius <note>Historically Emperor Aurelian.</note> of having advised her to rebel against the latter—a crime for which Longinus, along with several others, was put to death by the Emperor in 273.

## M.

<term>Macrocosm</term> (*Gr.*) Literally, the "Great Universe" or Cosmos.
<term>Magic</term>. The "great" science. According to Deveria and other Orientalists, "Magic was considered a sacred science inseparable from religion" by the oldest, most civilized, and learned nations. The Egyptians, for instance, were a most sincerely religious nation, as were—and still are—the Hindus. "Magic consists of, and is acquired by, the worship of the gods," says Plato. Could a nation that is proven to have firmly believed in magic for thousands of years, based on the irrefutable evidence of inscriptions and papyri, have been deceived for such a long time? And is it likely that generations upon generations of a learned and pious hierarchy—many of whom led lives of self-martyrdom, holiness, and asceticism—would have gone on deceiving themselves and the people (or even just the people) for the pleasure of perpetuating a belief in "miracles"? Fanatics, we are told, will do anything to enforce belief in their god or idols. To this, we reply:—

    Let's assemble.<meta>This page continues the entry for "Magic," transitioning from the discussion on page 344 regarding the sincerity of ancient hierarchies and their belief in the sacred sciences.</meta>

<page-num>345</page-num>
<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

anything. In such cases, Brahmans and Egyptian *Rekhget-amens* or Hierophants would not have popularized the belief in the power of man, through magic practices, to command the services of the gods. These gods are, in truth, only the occult powers or potencies of Nature, personified by the learned priests themselves, who reverenced in them only the attributes of the one unknown and nameless Principle. As Proclus, the Platonist, ably puts it:

> “Ancient priests, when they considered that there is a certain alliance and sympathy in natural things to each other, and of things manifest to occult powers, and discovered that all things subsist in all, fabricated a sacred science from this mutual sympathy and similarity . . . . and applied for occult purposes both celestial and earthly natures, by means of which, through a certain similarity, they drew down divine natures into this inferior abode.”

Magic is the science of communicating with and directing supernal supramundane powers, as well as commanding those of lower spheres; it is a practical knowledge of the hidden mysteries of nature which are known only to the few, because they are so difficult to acquire without falling into sin against the law. Ancient and medieval mystics divided magic into three classes—Theurgia, Goetia, and Natural Magic.

> “Theurgia has long since been appropriated as the peculiar sphere of the Theosophists and metaphysicians,” says Kenneth Mackenzie. “Goetia is black magic, and ‘natural’ or white magic has risen with healing in its wings to the proud position of an exact and progressive study.”

The remarks added by our late learned brother <note>Referring to Kenneth Mackenzie.</note> are remarkable:

> “The realistic desires of modern times have contributed to bring magic into disrepute and ridicule. . . . Faith (in oneself) is an essential element in magic, and existed long before other ideas which presume its pre-existence. It is said that it takes a wise man to make a fool; and a man’s idea must be exalted almost to madness, *that is*, his brain susceptibilities must be increased far beyond the low miserable status of modern civilization, before he can become a true magician, for a pursuit of this science implies a certain amount of isolation and an

<summary>This page explains that ancient magic was a sacred science based on the "sympathy" between natural and occult powers, distinguishing between Theurgia, Goetia, and Natural Magic. It highlights that true magic requires self-faith and a mental state far removed from modern materialism.</summary>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

self-denial.” It is certainly a very great isolation, the achievement of which constitutes a wonderful phenomenon—a miracle in itself. Moreover, magic is not something supernatural. As explained by Iamblichus, “they, through priestly <term>theurgy</term>, announce that they are able to ascend to *more elevated and universal essences*, and to those that are established above fate—namely, to god and the <term>demiurgos</term>: neither employing matter, nor assuming any other things besides, except the observation of a sensible time.” Already some are beginning to recognize the existence of subtle powers and influences in nature, of which they have hitherto known nothing. But, as Dr. Carter Blake truly remarks, “the nineteenth century is not that which has observed the genesis of new, nor the completion of old, methods of thought”; to which Mr. Bonwick adds, that “if the Ancients knew but little of our mode of investigation into the secrets of Nature, we know still less of their mode of research.”

**Magic, Black** (*see above*). Sorcery, abuse of powers.

**Magic, Ceremonial.** Magic performed according to <term>Kabalistic</term> rites, as alleged by the <term>Rosicrucians</term> and other mystics, by invoking powers spiritually higher than Man, and commanding <term>Elementals</term> who are far lower than himself on the scale of being.

**Magic, White,** or “Beneficent Magic,” as it is called, is *divine magic*, devoid of selfishness, love of power, ambition, or profit, and focused only on doing good to the world in general and one's neighbor in particular. The smallest attempt to use one's abnormal powers for self-gratification turns these powers into sorcery or Black Magic.

**Mahamanvantara** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, the great interludes between the <term>Manus</term>—the period of universal activity. <term>Manvantara</term> here implies simply a period of activity as opposed to <term>Pralaya</term> or rest—without reference to the length of the cycle.

**Mahat** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, “The Great One.” The first principle of Universal Intelligence and consciousness. In the <term>Puranic</term> philosophy, it is the first product of root-nature or <term>Pradhana</term> (the same as <term>Mulaprakriti</term>);

**Mahatma** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, "Great Soul." An adept of the highest order. An exalted being who, having attained mastery over his lower principles, is therefore living unimpeded by the "man of flesh." Mahatmas possess knowledge and power commensurate with the stage they have reached in their spiritual evolution. Called in Pali *Rahats* and *Arthas*.

**Mahayana** (*Sanskrit*) A school of Buddhist philosophy; literally, the "Great Vehicle." A mystical system founded by Nagarjuna. Its books were written in the second century B.C.

**Manas** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, the "Mind." The mental faculty which makes a man an intelligent and moral being, and distinguishes him from a mere animal; a synonym of <term>Mahat</term>. Esoterically, however, when unspecified, it means the Higher Ego or the sentient reincarnating Principle in man. When specified, it is called by Theosophists <term>Buddhi-Manas</term>, or the spiritual soul, in contradistinction to its human reflection—<term>Kama-Manas</term>.

**Manasaputra** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, the "Sons of Mind" or mind-born Sons; a name given to our Higher Egos before they incarnated into mankind. In the exoteric, though allegorical and symbolic, <term>Puranas</term> (the sacred and ancient writings of the Hindus), it is the title given to the mind-born Sons of <term>Brahma</term>, the *Kumara*.

**Manas Sutratma** (*Sanskrit*) Two words meaning "mind" (*Manas*) and "Thread Soul" (*Sutratma*). It is, as stated, the synonym of our Ego, or that which reincarnates. It is a technical term of Vedantic philosophy.

**Manas Taijasi** (*Sanskrit*) Literally, the "radiant" <term>Manas</term>; a state of the Higher Ego which only high metaphysicians are able to realize and comprehend. The same as "<term>Buddhi Taijasi</term>," which see.

**Manu** (*Sanskrit*) The great Indian legislator. The name comes from the Sanskrit root *man*, to think; MAN really stands only for Swayambhuva, the first of the Manus, who originated from *Swayambhu*, the Self-Existent, who is therefore the Logos and the progenitor of mankind. Manu is the first legislator—almost a divine being.

**Manvantara** (*Sanskrit*) A period of manifestation, as opposed to Pralaya (dissolution or rest); the term is applied to various cycles, especially to a Day of Brahmâ—4,320,000,000 Solar years—and to the reign of one Manu—308,448,000. Literally, Manuantara—“between Manus.” (See *Secret Doctrine*, Vol. II. p. 68, *et seq.*)

**Master.** A translation from the Sanskrit *Guru*, “Spiritual teacher,” and adopted by the Theosophists to designate the Adepts, from whom they receive their teachings.

**Materializations.** In Spiritualism, the word signifies the objective appearance of the so-called “spirits of the dead,” who occasionally re-clothe themselves in matter; that is, they form for themselves, out of the materials at hand found in the atmosphere and the emanations of those present, a temporary body bearing the human likeness of the deceased as he appeared when alive. Theosophists accept the phenomenon of “materialization,” but they reject the theory that it is produced by “Spirits,” meaning the immortal principles of disembodied persons. Theosophists hold that when the phenomena are genuine—which is a fact of rarer occurrence than is generally believed—they are produced by the *larvæ*, the eidolons, or Kamalokic “ghosts” of the dead personalities. (See “Kamaloka” and “Kamarupa.”) As Kamaloka is on the earth-plane and differs from it in materiality only by its degree of consciousness—which is why it is concealed from our normal sight—the occasional appearance of such shells is as natural as that of electric balls and other atmospheric phenomena. It is similar with the eidolons of the dead. They are present around us, but because they are on another plane, they do not see us any more than we see them. But whenever the strong desires of living men and the conditions provided by the abnormal constitutions of mediums are combined, these eidolons are drawn—even pulled down—from their plane onto ours and made objective. This is necromancy; it does no good for the dead and causes great harm to the living, in addition to the fact that it interferes with a law of nature. The occasional materialization of the “astral bodies” or doubles of living persons is quite another matter. These “astrals” are often mistaken for the apparitions of the dead since, like chameleons, our own “elementaries” along with those of the disembodied and cosmic Elementals will often take on the appearance of those images that are strongest in our thoughts. In short, at the so-called “materialization séances,” it is those present and the medium who create the specific apparition. Independent “apparitions” belong to another kind of psychic phenomenon.

**Materialist.** Not necessarily only one who believes in neither God nor soul, nor the survival of the soul, but also any person who materializes the purely spiritual. This includes those who believe in an anthropomorphic deity, in a soul capable of burning in hellfire, and a hell and paradise as physical locations instead of states of consciousness. American “Substantialists,” a Christian sect, are materialists, as are the so-called Spiritualists.

**Maya** (*Sans.*) Illusion; the cosmic power which makes phenomenal existence and the perceptions of it possible. In Hindu philosophy, only that which is changeless and eternal is called reality; all that which is subject to change through decay and differentiation, and which,

**Mediumship.** A word now accepted to describe that abnormal psycho-physiological state which leads a person to mistake the fantasies of their imagination or their hallucinations—whether real or artificial—for reality. No person who is entirely healthy on the physiological and psychic planes can ever be a medium. What mediums see, hear, and feel is “real” but untrue; it is either gathered from the astral plane, which is so deceptive in its vibrations and suggestions, or from pure hallucinations that have no actual existence except for the person perceiving them. “Mediumship” is a kind of popularized *mediatorship* in which a person afflicted with this faculty is supposed to become an intermediary between a living person and a departed “Spirit.” There are established methods of training for the development of this undesirable skill.

**Mercavah**, or *Mercabah* (Hebrew). “A chariot. The Kabbalists say that after the Supreme Being had established the ten Sephiroth—which, in their totality, form Adam Kadmon, the Archetypal Man—He used them as a chariot or throne of glory to descend into the souls of humanity.”

**Mesmerism.** The term comes from Mesmer, who rediscovered this magnetic force and its practical application around the year 1775 in Vienna. It is a vital current that one person may transfer to another, through which he induces an abnormal state of the nervous system that allows him to have a direct influence upon the mind and will of the subject or mesmerized person.

**Metaphysics.** From the Greek *meta* (beyond) and *physica* (the things of the external material world). To translate it as “beyond nature” or “supernatural” is to focus on the literal meaning and ignore the spirit; it is more accurately described as being beyond the natural, visible, or concrete. In ontology and philosophy, metaphysics is the term used to designate the science that treats real and permanent being as contrasted with unreal, illusory, or phenomenal being.

# GLOSSARY.

**Microcosm.** The “little” Universe, meaning man, who is made in the image of his creator, the Macrocosm, or “great” Universe, and contains all that the latter contains. These terms are used in <term>Occultism</term> and <term>Theosophy</term>.

**Mishnah** (*Hebrew*). Literally, “a repetition,” from the word *Shânâh*, “to repeat” something said orally. A summary of written explanations from the oral traditions of the Jews and a digest of the Scriptures on which the later <term>Talmud</term> was based.

**Moksha** (*Sanskrit*). The same as <term>Nirvana</term>; a state of rest and bliss for the “Soul-pilgrim” after death.

**Monad.** It is Unity, the ONE; but in occultism, it often means the unified duad, <term>Atma-Buddhi</term>—that immortal part of man which, by incarnating in the lower kingdoms and gradually progressing through them to the level of Man, finds its way from there to the final goal—<term>Nirvana</term>.

**Monas** (*Greek*). The same as the Latin <term>Monad</term>; “the only,” a Unit. In the Pythagorean system, the Duad emanates from the higher and solitary Monas, which is thus the First Cause.

**Monogenes** (*Greek*). Literally, the “only-begotten”; a name of Proserpine and other gods and goddesses, as well as Jesus.

**Mundakya Upanishad** (*Sanskrit*). Literally, the “Mundaka esoteric doctrine.” A work of great antiquity; it was translated by Raja Ram Mohun Roy.

**Mysteries** (*Sacred*). They were performed in ancient temples by the initiated <term>Hierophants</term> for the benefit and instruction of candidates. The most solemn and occult were certainly those performed in Egypt by “the band of secret-keepers,” as Mr. Bonwick calls the <term>Hierophants</term>. Maurice describes their nature very graphically in a few lines. Speaking of the <term>Mysteries</term> performed in Philæ (the island in the Nile), he says: “It was in these gloomy caverns that the grand mystic arcana of the goddess (Isis) were unfolded to the adoring aspirant, while the solemn hymn of initiation resounded through the long extent

<meta>This page continues the glossary definitions for the term "Mysteries," followed by "Mystery Language," "Mystic," and "Mysticism."</meta>

# GLOSSARY.

of these stony recesses." The word "mystery" is derived from the Greek *muô*, meaning "to close the mouth," and every symbol connected with them had a hidden meaning. As Plato and many of the other sages of antiquity affirm, these mysteries were highly religious, moral, and beneficent as a school of ethics. The Greek Mysteries, those of Ceres and Bacchus, were only imitations of the Egyptian ones, and the author of "Egyptian Belief and Modern Thought" informs us that our own word "chapel" or *capella* is said to be the *caph-el* or college of *El*, the solar divinity." The well-known Kabeiri are associated with the mysteries.

In short, the Mysteries were in every country a series of dramatic performances, in which the mysteries of Cosmogony and nature in general were personified by the priests and neophytes, who played the parts of various gods and goddesses, reenacting supposed scenes (allegories) from their respective lives. These were explained in their hidden meaning to the candidates for initiation and incorporated into philosophical doctrines.

**Mystery Language.** The priestly secret "jargon" used by the initiated priests and employed only when discussing sacred things. Every nation had its own "mystery" tongue, unknown to all except those admitted to the Mysteries.

**Mystic.** From the Greek word *mysticos*. In antiquity, it referred to someone belonging to those admitted to the ancient mysteries; in our own times, it refers to one who practices mysticism or holds mystic, transcendental views, etc.

**Mysticism.** Any doctrine involved in mystery and metaphysics, dealing more with ideal worlds than with our matter-of-fact, actual universe.

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

### N.

**Nazarene Codex.** The scriptures of both the Nazarenes and the Nabotheans. According to various Church Fathers, especially Jerome and Epiphanius, these were heretical teachings; however, they are actually one of the many Gnostic interpretations of cosmogony and theogony that led to a distinct sect.

**Necromancy.** The summoning of the images of the dead, considered in both antiquity and by modern occultists as a practice of Black Magic. Iamblichus, Porphyry, and other theurgists disapproved of the practice just as much as Moses, who condemned the “witches” of his day to death—these witches often being merely mediums, such as the case of the Witch of Endor and Samuel.

**Neoplatonists.** A school of philosophy that emerged between the second and third centuries of our era, founded by Ammonius Saccas of Alexandria. They were identical to the Philalethians and the Analogeticists; they were also called Theurgists and by various other names. They were the Theosophists of the early centuries. Neoplatonism is Platonic philosophy *plus ecstasy*, or divine Raja-yoga.

**Nephesh (Heb.)** “Breath of Life, *Anima, Mens Vitæ* (Mind of Life), and appetites. The term is used very loosely in the Bible. It generally means Prana, or ‘life’; in the Kabbalah, it represents animal passions and the animal soul.” Therefore, as maintained in Theosophical teachings, *Nephesh* is the Prana-Kamic Principle, or the vital animal soul in humans.

**Nirmanakaya (Sans.)** In esoteric philosophy, this is something entirely different from its popular meaning and from the theories of Orientalists. Some call the Nirmanakaya body “Nirvana with remains” (Schlagintweit), probably on the assumption that it is a type of Nirvanic state where consciousness and *form* are retained. Others say it is one of the *Trikaya* (three bodies) with “the power

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

...of assuming any form of appearance in order to propagate <term>Buddhism</term>” (<term>Eitel's</term> idea); or again, that “it is the incarnate <term>avatara</term> of a deity” (<term>ibid.</term>). <term>Occultism</term>, on the other hand, says (in the “<term>Voice of the Silence</term>”) that <term>Nirmanakaya</term>, although literally meaning a transformed “body,” is a state. The form is that of the <term>Adept</term> or <term>Yogi</term> who enters, or chooses, that <term>post-mortem</term> condition in preference to the <term>Dharmakaya</term> or absolute <term>Nirvanic</term> state. He does this because the latter <term>Kaya</term> separates him forever from the world of form, conferring upon him a state of *selfish bliss* in which no other living being can participate; the adept is thus precluded from the possibility of helping humanity, or even <term>devas</term>. As a <term>Nirmanakaya</term>, however, the adept leaves behind only his physical body and retains every other “principle” except the <term>Karmic</term>, for he has crushed this out forever from his nature during life, and it can never resurrect in his <term>post-mortem</term> state. Thus, instead of entering into selfish bliss, he chooses a life of self-sacrifice—an existence that ends only with the life-cycle—in order to be able to help mankind in an invisible yet most effective manner. (See “<term>Voice of the Silence</term>,” third <term>Treatise</term>, “The <term>Seven Portals</term>.”) Thus, a <term>Nirmanakaya</term> is not, as is popularly believed, the body “in which a <term>Buddha</term> or a <term>Bodhisattva</term> appears on earth,” but truly one who—whether a <term>Chutuktu</term> or a <term>Khubilkhan</term>, an adept or a <term>Yogi</term> during life—has since become a member of that invisible Host which forever protects and watches over humanity within <term>Karmic</term> limits. Often mistaken for a “<term>Spirit</term>,” a <term>Deva</term>, <term>God</term> himself, etc., a <term>Nirmanakaya</term> is always a protecting, compassionate, and truly a guardian angel to him who is worthy of his help. Whatever objection may be brought against this doctrine, and however much it is denied—simply because it has never been made public in Europe before and is therefore unknown to <term>Orientalists</term>, leading them to call it a “myth of modern invention”—no one will be bold enough to say that this idea of helping suffering mankind at the price of one’s own almost interminable self-sacrifice is not one of the grandest and noblest ever evolved from the human brain.

--- Page 371 ---
<meta>This page continues the glossary definitions, transitioning from the discussion of the Nirmanakaya state to the definitions of Nirvana and Nirvanee, clarifying the esoteric meaning of these terms against common scholarly interpretations.</meta>

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

<term>Nirvana</term> (Sanskrit) According to <term>Orientalists</term>, it is the entire “blowing-out,” like the flame of a candle—the utter extinction of existence. However, in exoteric (public) explanations, it is the state of absolute existence and absolute consciousness into which the <term>Ego</term> of a person who reached the highest degree of perfection and holiness during life enters after the body dies, and occasionally—as is the case with <term>Gautama Buddha</term> and others—during life.

<term>Nirvanee</term> (Sanskrit) One who has attained <term>Nirvana</term>—an emancipated <term>Soul</term>. Every scholar who has visited <term>India</term>, <term>China</term>, or <term>Japan</term> is well aware that *Nirvana* means something quite different from the simplistic assertions of <term>Orientalists</term>. It is an “escape from misery,” but only from the misery of matter; it is freedom from *<term>Klesha</term>* (affliction) or *<term>Kama</term>* (desire), and the complete extinction of animal desires. If we are told that the *<term>Abhidharma</term>* defines <term>Nirvana</term> as “a state of absolute <term>annihilation</term>,” we agree, but add to the last word the qualification “of everything connected with matter or the physical world.” This is simply because the latter (as well as everything in it) is an illusion or *<term>Maya</term>*. <term>Sakyamuni Buddha</term> said in the last moments of his life: “The spiritual body is immortal.” (See the “*<term>Sanskrit-Chinese Dictionary</term>*.”) As <term>Mr. Eitel</term>, the scholarly <term>Sinologist</term>, explains: “The popular exoteric systems agree in defining *Nirvana* *negatively* as a state of absolute exemption from the circle of <term>transmigration</term>; as a state of entire freedom from all forms of existence—beginning with freedom from all passion and exertion—and a state of indifference to all sensibility.” He might have added: “the death of all compassion for the world of suffering.” This is why the <term>Bodhisattvas</term> who prefer the *<term>Nirmanakaya</term>* to the *<term>Dharmakaya</term>* vesture stand higher in popular estimation than the *<term>Nirvanees</term>*. But the same scholar adds that, “*Positively* (and esoterically), they define *Nirvana* as the highest state of spiritual bliss—as absolute immortality through the absorption of the <term>Soul</term> (<term>Spirit</term>, rather) into itself, while preserving *<term>individuality</term>*, so that, for example, <term>Buddhas</term>, after entering <term>Nirvana</term>, may reappear on earth—that is, in the future *<term>Manvantara</term>*.”

<vocab>Nirvana, Orientalists, Gautama Buddha, Nirvanee, Soul, India, China, Japan, Klesha, Kama, Abhidharma, Maya, Sakyamuni Buddha, Sanskrit-Chinese Dictionary, Mr. Eitel, Sinologist, transmigration, Bodhisattvas, Nirmanakaya, Dharmakaya, Spirit, Buddhas, Manvantara, Ego, Annihilation, Individuality</vocab>

<summary>This page defines Nirvana and Nirvanee, clarifying that while scholars often describe Nirvana as total extinction, esoteric philosophy views it as absolute spiritual bliss and immortality that preserves individuality. It explains that true Nirvana is the annihilation of material ties and animal desires, rather than the soul itself.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the glossary definitions, moving from terms related to Nirvana and the spiritual body into Greek, Egyptian, and esoteric concepts regarding the mind, the essential nature of reality, and the definition of the occult sciences.</meta>

->## GLOSSARY.<-

**Noumena** (*Greek*) The true essential nature of Being as distinguished from the illusive objects of the senses.

**Nous** (*Greek*) A Platonic term for the Higher Mind or Soul. It means Spirit as distinct from the animal-Soul, *Psyche*; divine consciousness or mind in man. The name was adopted by the Gnostics for their first conscious Æon, which, according to Occultists, is the third *logos* cosmically, and the third "principle" (from above) or Manas in man. (See below, "Nout.")

**Nout** (*Egyptian*) In the Egyptian Pantheon, it meant the "One-only-One," because in popular or exoteric religion, it does not proceed higher than the *third manifestation* which radiates from the *Unknowable* and the *Unknown* in the esoteric philosophy of every nation. The *Nous* of Anaxagoras was the *Mahat* of the Hindus—*Brahmâ*, the *first manifested* deity—"the Mind or spirit Self-potent." This creative principle is the *primum mobile* of everything to be found in the Universe—its Soul or Ideation. (See "Seven Principles" in man.)

->### O.<-

**Occultism**. See OCCULT SCIENCES.

**Occult Sciences**. The science of the secrets of nature—physical and psychic, mental and spiritual; called Hermetic and Esoteric Sciences. In the West, the Kabbala may be named; in the East, mysticism, magic, and Yoga philosophy. The latter is often referred to by the *Chelas* in India as the *seventh "Darshana"* (school of philosophy), as there are only *six Darshanas* in India known to the world of the uninitiated. These sciences are, and have been for ages, hidden from the common people, for the very good reason that they would never be appreciated by the selfish educated classes, who would misuse them for their own profit, and thus turn the

<summary>This page of the glossary defines philosophical and spiritual terms including Noumena, Nous, Nout, and the Occult Sciences, linking Greek and Egyptian concepts to Hindu and esoteric philosophy. It describes the Occult Sciences as the study of nature's physical and spiritual secrets, traditionally kept hidden from the uninitiated to prevent misuse.</summary>
<keywords>Noumena, Nous, Nout, Occultism, Occult Sciences, Gnostics, Anaxagoras, Mahat, Brahmâ, Darshana, Esoteric Sciences, Kabbala</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the definition of "Occult Sciences," specifically addressing the use of specialized jargon in esoteric studies and providing definitions for terms starting with "O."</meta>

<page-num>357</page-num>
<header>GLOSSARY. 357</header>

Divine science into *black magic*, nor by the uneducated, who would not understand them. It is often raised as an accusation against the Esoteric Philosophy of the Kabbala that its literature is full of "a barbarous and meaningless jargon," unintelligible to the ordinary mind. But do not *exact sciences*—medicine, physiology, chemistry, and the rest—plead guilty to the same charge? Do not official scientists veil their facts and discoveries with a newly coined and most barbarous Greco-Latin terminology? As justly remarked by our late brother, Kenneth Mackenzie, "to juggle with words in this way, when the facts are so simple, is the art of the scientists of the present time, in striking contrast to those of the seventeenth century, who called spades spades, and not 'agricultural implements.'" Moreover, while their "facts" would be as simple and as comprehensible if expressed in ordinary language, the facts of Occult Science are of such an abstruse nature that in most cases no words exist in European languages to express them. Finally, our "jargon" is a *double necessity*—(a) for describing these facts clearly to one who is versed in occult terminology; and (b) for concealing them from the uninitiated.

**Occultist.** One who practices Occultism, an adept in the Secret Sciences; the term is also very often applied to a mere student.

**Occult World.** The name of the first book that treated of theosophy, its history, and certain of its tenets. Written by A. P. Sinnett, then editor of the leading Indian newspaper, the *Pioneer*, of Allahabad, India.

**Olympiodorus.** The last Neoplatonist of fame and renown in the school of Alexandria. He lived in the sixth century under the Emperor Justinian. There were several writers and philosophers of this name in both pre-Christian and post-Christian periods. One of these was the teacher of Proclus, another a historian in the eighth century, and so on.

**Origen.** A Christian churchman, born at the end of the second century, probably in Africa, of whom little, if anything, is known.

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

since his biographical fragments have passed to posterity on the authority of Eusebius, the most unmitigated falsifier who has ever existed in any age. The latter is credited with having collected more than one hundred letters of Origen (or Origenes Adamantius), which are now said to have been lost. To Theosophists, the most interesting of all the works of Origen is his “Doctrine of the Pre-existence of Souls.” He was a pupil of Ammonius Saccas, and for a long time attended the lectures of this great teacher of philosophy.

### P.

**Panænus.** A Platonic philosopher in the Alexandrian school of the Philalethians.

**Pandora.** In Greek Mythology, the first woman on earth, created by Vulcan out of clay to punish Prometheus and counteract his gift to mortals. After each god gave her a gift of some virtue, she was made to carry them in a box to Prometheus, who, however, being endowed with foresight, sent her away, changing the gifts into evils. Thus, when his brother Epimretheus saw and married her, and when he opened the box, all the evils now afflicting humanity issued from it, and they have remained in the world ever since.

**Pantheist.** One who identifies God with nature and *vice versa*. If we must regard Deity as an infinite and omnipresent Principle, this can hardly be otherwise; nature is thus simply the physical aspect of Deity, or its body.

**Parabrahm** (*Sanskrit*) A Vedantin term meaning “beyond Brahmâ.” The Supreme and absolute Principle, impersonal and nameless. In the Veda, it is referred to as “THAT.”

**Paranirvana.** In the Vedantic philosophy, the highest form of nirvana—beyond the latter.

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical glossary of Theosophical and philosophical terms, moving from the letter "P" entries for Panænus and Parabrahm to definitions for Parsees, Personality, and Philo the Jew.</meta>

<page-num>359</page-num>
<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

**Parsees** (or Parsis). The present Persian followers of Zoroaster <note>an ancient Persian prophet</note>, now settled in India, especially in Mumbai <note>original: Bombay</note> and Gujarat <note>original: Guzerat</note>. They are sun and fire worshippers and represent one of the most intelligent and esteemed communities in the country, generally occupied with commercial pursuits. There are between 50,000 and 60,000 remaining in India, where they settled some 1,000 years ago.

**Personality.** The teachings of Occultism divide humanity into three aspects: the divine, the thinking or rational, and the irrational or animal man. For metaphysical purposes, the human being is also considered under a seven-fold <note>original: septenary</note> division, or, as it is agreed to express it in Theosophy, he is composed of seven "principles." Three of these constitute the *Higher Triad* <note>a group of three</note>, and the remaining four make up the *lower Quaternary* <note>original: Quarternary; a group of four</note>. It is in the latter that the *Personality* dwells, which embraces all the characteristics—including memory and consciousness—of each physical life in turn. The *Individuality* is the Higher Self <note>original: Ego (Latin: "I")</note>, or the *Mind* <note>original: Manas (Sanskrit)</note>, of the Triad considered as a single Unity. In other words, the Individuality is our imperishable *Self* <note>original: Ego</note> which reincarnates and clothes itself in a *new Personality* at every new birth.

**Phallic Worship,** or Sex Worship; the reverence and adoration shown to those gods and goddesses who, like Shiva <note>original: Siva</note> and Durga in India, symbolize the two sexes respectively.

**Philadelphians.** Literally, "those who love their brother-man" <note>from the Greek words 'philos' (loving) and 'adelphos' (brother)</note>. A sect in the seventeenth century founded by a woman named Jane Leadly. They objected to all rites, forms, or ceremonies of the Church, and even to the Church itself; instead, they professed to be guided in soul and spirit by an internal Deity—their own *Self* <note>original: Ego</note> or *God* within them.

**Philalethians.** <note>Lovers of Truth; from the Greek 'philos' (loving) and 'aletheia' (truth)</note>. (See "Neoplatonists.")

**Philo the Jew.** <note>original: Philo-Judæus</note> A Hellenized <note>influenced by Greek culture and language</note> Jew of Alexandria, a famous historian and philosopher of the first century. He was born about the year 30 B.C. and died between the years 45 and 50 A.D. Philo's symbolic interpretation of the Bible is very remarkable. The animals, birds, reptiles, trees, and places mentioned in it are all, it is said, "allegories of conditions of the soul, of

**Philosopher’s Stone.** A term in alchemy; also called the *Powder of Projection*, a mysterious “principle” having the power of transforming base metals into pure gold. In Theosophy, it symbolizes the transformation of the lower animal nature of man into the highest divine nature.

**Phren.** A Pythagorean term denoting what we call the Kama-manas, which is still overshadowed by Buddhi-Manas.

**Plane.** From the Latin *Planus* (level, flat), an extension of space, whether in the physical or metaphysical sense. In occultism, the range or extent of some state of consciousness, or the state of matter corresponding to the perceptive powers of a particular set of senses or the action of a particular force.

**Planetary Spirits.** Rulers and governors of the planets; planetary gods.

**Plastic.** Used in occultism in reference to the nature and essence of the astral body, or the “Protean Soul.” (See “Plastic Soul” in the *Theosophical Glossary*.)

**Pleroma.** “Fullness”; a Gnostic term also used by Saint Paul. The divine world or the abode of gods; universal space divided into metaphysical Aeons.

**Plotinus.** A distinguished Platonic philosopher of the third century, a great practical mystic renowned for his virtues and learning. He taught a doctrine identical with that of the Vedantins—namely, that the spirit-soul, emanating from the One Divine Principle, was reunited to it after its pilgrimage on earth. (See *Theosophical Glossary*.)

<header>GLOSSARY.</header>

**Porphyry (Porphyrius).** His real name was *Malek*, which led to his being considered a Jew. He came from Tyre, and after first studying under Longinus, the eminent philosopher-critic, he became the disciple of Plotinus in Rome. He was a Neo-Platonist and a distinguished writer, especially famous for his controversy with Iamblichus regarding the evils attending the practice of Theurgy; however, he was eventually converted to his opponent's views. A natural-born mystic, he followed, like his master Plotinus, the pure Indian Raja-Yoga system, which, through training, leads to the union of the soul with the over-soul of the universe, and of the human with its *divine* soul, Buddhi-Manas. He complained, however, that despite all his efforts, he reached the highest state of ecstasy only once, and that was when he was sixty-eight years of age, while his teacher Plotinus had experienced the supreme bliss six times during his life. (*See* “Porphyry,” in the *Theosophical Glossary*.)

**Pot Amun.** A Coptic term meaning “one consecrated to the god Amun,” the Wisdom-god. The name of an Egyptian priest and occultist under the Ptolemies.

**Pragna, or Prajna** (*Sans.*) A term used to designate the “Universal Mind.” A synonym for *Mahat*.

**Pralaya** (*Sans.*) Dissolution; the opposite of Manvantara. One is the period of rest and the other is the period of full activity (death and life) of a planet, or of the whole universe.

**Prana** (*Sans.*) Life Principle; the breath of life, *Nephesh*.

**Protean Soul.** A name for *Mayavi rupa* or the thought-body; the higher astral form which assumes any and all forms at the will of an adept’s thought. (*See* “Plastic Soul” in the *Theosophical Glossary*.)

**Psychism.** The word is used now to denote every kind of mental phenomena, such as mediumship as well as the higher form of sensitivity. A newly-coined word.

**Purânas** (*Sans.*) Literally, “the ancient,” referring to Hindu writings or Scriptures, of which there is a considerable number.

**Pythagoras.** The most famous mystic philosopher, born at Samos about 586 B.C., who taught the heliocentric system and reincarnation, the highest mathematics, and the highest metaphysics, and who established a school famous throughout the world. (See *Theosophical Glossary* for more details.)

### Q.

**Quaternary.** The four lower "principles in man," those which constitute his personality (that is, the Body, Astral Double, *Prana* <note>The OCR reads "Prama," which is a typographical error for "Prana," the life principle defined on the previous page.</note> or life, organs of desire, and lower *Manas*, or brain-mind), as distinguished from the Higher Ternary or Triad, which is composed of the higher Spiritual Soul, Mind, and Atman (Higher Self).

### R.

**Recollection, Remembrance, Reminiscence.** Occultists distinguish between these three functions. However, because a glossary cannot contain a full explanation of every term in all its metaphysical and subtle differences, we can only state here that these terms vary in their application, according to whether they relate to a past or present birth, and whether one or the other of these phases of memory emanates from the spiritual or the material brain; or, again, from the "Individuality" or the "Personality."
**Reincarnation,** or **Re-birth;** the once universal doctrine which taught that the Ego is born on this earth an innumerable number of times. Nowadays it is denied by Christians, who seem to misunderstand the teachings of their own gospels. Nevertheless, the periodic taking on of flesh throughout long cycles by the higher human
